and Section
A.3
. For instance, consider Figure
9d
, which is the neighborhood of
h_{jj}
for
2\leq j\leq n-1
,
j-1,j\in\Gamma_{2}
,
i:=\gamma^{*}(j-1)+1
and
\gamma|_{\Delta(i)\cap\Gamma_{1}}<0
. We see that

\begin{split}\deg y(h_{jj})=&(\deg h_{j,j+1}-\deg h_{in})-(\deg h_{j-1,j}-\deg h_{j-1,j-1})-\\
&-(\deg h_{j+1,j+1}-\deg h_{i-1,n})=2-1-1=0.\end{split}

(4.50)

Thus the action
(a,U)\mapsto a\cdot U
,
a\in\mathbb{C}^{*}
,
U\in\GL_{n}
induces a global toric action of rank
1
upon
\mathcal{GC}_{h}^{\dagger}(\mathbf{\Gamma},\GL_{n})
.
∎

Proposition 4.22
.

Let
\psi
be any variable from the initial extended cluster of
\mathcal{GC}^{\dagger}_{h}(\mathbf{\Gamma})
. Then the following identities hold:

1)

If
\psi
is a
\varphi
- or
c
-function, then
\mathcal{G}^{*}(\psi(U))=\psi(U)
;

2)

If
\psi=h_{ij}
for some
h
-function
h_{ij}(U)
, then

\mathcal{G}^{*}(h_{ij}(U))=\begin{cases}\tilde{h}_{ij}(U)\tilde{h}_{q+1,q+1}(U)\ &\text{if}\ p=\gamma^{\nu}(i-1)\ \text{for some}\ \nu>0;\\
\tilde{h}_{ij}(U)\ &\text{otherwise.}\end{cases}

(4.51)

Moreover,
\mathcal{G}
is a birational quasi-isomorphism.

Proof.

Due to the invariance properties of
\varphi
- and
c
-functions (see (
1.6
) and (
1.7
)) and the fact that
G(U)
is a unipotent lower triangular matrix, we see that Part
1
holds. For the second part, we see that for any
j\in[1,n]
and
I\subseteq[1,n]
,
|I|=n-j+1
,

\det[\mathcal{F}(\mathcal{G}(U))]_{I}^{[j,n]}=\det[\tilde{\mathcal{F}}(U)]_{I}^{[j,n]}.

We also notice that if
S^{\gamma}(\alpha_{0})
is a
\gamma
-string such that
q\notin S^{\gamma}(\alpha_{0})
, then
S^{\gamma}(\alpha_{0})=S^{\theta}(\alpha_{0})
; otherwise, if
q\in S^{\gamma}(\alpha_{0})
, then
S^{\gamma}(\alpha_{0})=S^{\theta}(\alpha_{0})\cup S^{\theta}(q)
. Now Part
2
follows from an application of
\mathcal{G}^{*}
to the definition of the
h
-functions given in (
1.11
).

The last statement can be proved either via Proposition
2.25
or Proposition
2.26
. Let us use the latter proposition, for it also shows that, as a quasi-isomorphism,
\mathcal{G}^{*}
is generated by toric actions when
|\Gamma_{1}\setminus\tilde{\Gamma}_{1}|=1
. First of all, by Lemma
5.10
, there are global toric action of rank
1
acting upon
\mathcal{GC}_{h}^{\dagger}(\mathbf{\Gamma},\GL_{n})
and
\mathcal{GC}_{h}^{\dagger}(\tilde{\mathbf{\Gamma}},\GL_{n})
induced by the polynomial degrees of the variables. From formula (
4.49
), we also see that

\deg h_{ij}-\deg\tilde{h}_{ij}=\begin{cases}\deg\tilde{h}_{q+1,q+1}\ &\text{if}\ p=\gamma^{\nu}(i-1)\ \text{for some}\ \nu>0;\\
0\ &\text{otherwise.}\end{cases}

(4.52)

It follows from Proposition
2.26
that
\mathcal{G}^{*}
is a quasi-isomorphism. Since
\mathcal{G}^{*}
induces an isomorphism between
\mathcal{O}(\GL_{n})[h_{q+1,q+1}^{\pm 1}]
and
\mathcal{O}(\GL_{n})[\tilde{h}_{q+1,q+1}^{\pm 1}]
, we conclude that it is a birational quasi-isomorphism.
∎

5
Proofs of Theorems
1.5
and
1.6
for
\mathcal{GC}_{h}^{\dagger}(\mathbf{\Gamma})

In this section, we prove Theorem
1.5
and Theorem
1.6
for
\mathcal{GC}_{h}^{\dagger}(\mathbf{\Gamma})
. Each subsection corresponds to one part of a theorem (note that Part
1
follows from Part
4
due to Part
2
of Proposition
2.16
). The main idea is to use an inductive argument on the size
|\Gamma_{1}|
along with the birational quasi-isomorphisms constructed in Section
4
. The statements for
|\Gamma_{1}|=0
follow from the earlier work
[
20
]
, the statements for
|\Gamma_{1}|=1
require a manual verification only for the marked variables, and the statements for
|\Gamma_{1}|>1
follow from the existence of complementary birational quasi-isomorphisms.

Conventions.

Throughout the section, when the indices of the
h
-variables are out of range, the following conventions are used:

\displaystyle h_{1j}(U):=\varphi_{j-1,n-j+1}(U)=(-1)^{\varepsilon_{1j}}\det U^{[j,n]}_{[1,n-j+1]},\

\displaystyle j\in[2,n];

(5.1)

\displaystyle h_{i,n+1}(U):=\begin{cases}h_{jj}(U)\ &\text{if}\ i-1\in\Gamma_{2}\\
1\ &\text{otherwise}\end{cases}\

\displaystyle i\in[1,n]

(5.2)

where
j:=\gamma^{*}(i-1)+1
if
i-1\in\Gamma_{2}
.

5.1
Completeness

In this subsection, we prove that
\mathcal{GC}_{h}^{\dagger}(\mathbf{\Gamma},\GL_{n})
and
\mathcal{GC}_{h}^{\dagger}(\mathbf{\Gamma},\SL_{n})
are complete. Along the way, we prove that all the variables in the initial extended cluster are irreducible (as elements of
\mathcal{O}(\GL_{n})
) and (for the initial cluster variables) coprime with their mutations. The final result of the subsection is Proposition
5.9
, which corresponds to Part
2
of Theorem
1.5
and Part
2
of Theorem
1.6
.

5.1.1
The case of
|\Gamma_{1}|=|\Gamma_{2}|=1
(shifted cluster).

Let
\mathbf{\Gamma}=(\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2},\gamma)
be a BD triple of type
A_{n-1}
such that
\Gamma_{1}:=\{p\}
and
\Gamma_{2}:=\{q\}
for some simple roots
p
and
q
. We decorate the
h
-variables in
\mathcal{GC}_{h}^{\dagger}(\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\text{std}})
with a tilde, to distinguish them from the
h
-variables in
\mathcal{GC}_{h}^{\dagger}(\mathbf{\Gamma})
. Recall that we choose the marker (see Definition
2.20
) between
\mathcal{GC}_{h}^{\dagger}(\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\text{std}})
and
\mathcal{GC}_{h}^{\dagger}(\mathbf{\Gamma})
in such a way that each
\varphi
- and
c
- variable are related to themselves, and each
h_{ij}
is related to
\tilde{h}_{ij}
. The only marked variable is given by
\psi_{\square}:=h_{q+1,q+1}
. The map
\mathcal{F}^{c}
assumes the form

\mathcal{F}^{c}(U)=\tilde{\gamma}^{*}(U_{-})U\tilde{\gamma}^{*}(U_{-})^{-1},\ \ \tilde{\gamma}^{*}(U_{-})=I+\alpha(U)e_{p+1,p},\ \ \alpha(U)=\frac{1}{\psi_{\square}(U)}\det U^{\{q\}\cup[q+2,n]}_{[q+1,n]}.

(5.3)

Shifting the initial cluster.

We will prove
\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbb{C}}(\mathcal{GC}_{h}^{\dagger}(\mathbf{\Gamma},\GL_{n}))=\mathcal{O}(\GL_{n})
starting not in the initial extended cluster, but in a more convenient one. Let us set
\Psi
to be the extended cluster in
\mathcal{GC}_{h}^{\dagger}(\mathbf{\Gamma})
obtained via mutating the initial extended cluster
\Psi_{0}
along the following mutation sequence:
17
17

17

If
p=n-1
, the mutation sequence is empty, so we set
\Psi
to be the initial extended cluster.

{h}_{p+1,n}\rightarrow{h}_{p+1,n-1}\rightarrow\cdots\rightarrow{h}_{p+1,p+2}.

(5.4)

We set
\tilde{\Psi}
to be the extended cluster in
\mathcal{GC}_{h}^{\dagger}(\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\text{std}})
related to
\Psi
(see Definition
2.23
) and
\Psi^{\prime}
to be the extended cluster in
\mathcal{GC}_{h}^{\dagger}(\mathbf{\Gamma})
obtained by mutating
\Psi
in the direction of
\psi_{\square}
.

Lemma 5.1
.

The extended cluster
\Psi
contains the variables

{h}^{\prime}_{p+1,n-s}(U)=-(-1)^{s(n-(p+1))}\det U_{\{p\}\cup[p+2,s+p+2]}^{[n-s-1,n]},\ \ s\in[0,n-p-2].

(5.5)

Moreover, for any
\psi\in\Psi\setminus\{h_{p+1,p+1}\}
and the related variable
\tilde{\psi}\in\tilde{\Psi}\setminus\{\tilde{h}_{p+1,p+1}\}
,
\tilde{\psi}(U)=\psi(U)
.

Proof.

It is well-known (see, for instance,
[
31
, Lemma 5.3]
, with adjustments of signs) that the related extended cluster
\tilde{\Psi}
contains the variables

{h}^{\prime}_{p+1,n-s}(U)=-(-1)^{s(n-(p+1))}\det U_{\{p\}\cup[p+2,s+p+2]}^{[n-s-1,n]},\ \ s\in[0,n-p-2].

(5.6)

Applying
(\mathcal{F}^{c})^{*}
to (
5.6
), we see that

(\mathcal{F}^{c})^{*}[\tilde{h}^{\prime}_{p+1,n-s}(U)]=-(-1)^{s(n-(p+1))}\det U_{\{p\}\cup[p+2,s+p+2]}^{[n-s-1,n]}.

(5.7)

Since
\mathcal{Q}
is a quasi-isomorphism, we conclude that
\Psi
contains the variables (
5.5
) as well. In particular,
\Psi
and
\tilde{\Psi}
differ only in the variable
h_{p+1,p+1}
.
∎

For the next lemma, let us mention that the frozen variable
h_{p+1,p+1}
in
\mathcal{GC}_{h}^{\dagger}(\mathbf{\Gamma})
is given by

h_{p+1,p+1}(U)=\det U^{[p+1,n]}_{[p+1,n]}\det U^{[q+1,n]}_{[q+1,n]}+\det U^{[p+1,n]}_{\{p\}\cup[p+2,n]}\det U^{\{q\}\cup[q+2,n]}_{[q+1,n]}.

(5.8)

Lemma 5.2
.

The mutation of
\Psi
at the marked variable
\psi_{\square}
produces the variable

\psi_{\square}^{\prime}(U)=(-1)^{\varepsilon_{\square}}\left[\det U^{[p+1,n]}_{[p+1,n]}\det U_{I_{\square}}^{[q+1,n]}+\det U^{[p+1,n]}_{\{p\}\cup[p+2,n]}\det U^{\{q\}\cup[q+2,n]}_{I_{\square}}\right]

(5.9)

where

\varepsilon_{\square}:=\begin{cases}n-q\ &\text{if}\ q-p\neq 1,\\
n-q+1\ &\text{if}\ q-p=1;\end{cases}\ \ \ \ I_{\square}:=\begin{cases}[q,n-1]\ &\text{if}\ q-p\neq 1,\\
\{q-1\}\cup[q+1,n-1]\ &\text{if}\ q-p=1.\end{cases}

(5.10)

Proof.

Case
q-p=1
,
q\leq n-2
.
The mutation relation for
\psi_{\square}
reads

\psi_{\square}\psi_{\square}^{\prime}=(h_{p+1,p+2}^{\prime})^{2}h_{q+1,q+2}+h_{p+1,p+3}^{\prime}h_{p+1,p+1}.

(5.11)

Expanding the right-hand side, we see that

\begin{split}(-1)^{n-p}&\psi_{\square}(U)\psi_{\square}^{\prime}(U)=\left[\det U^{[p+1,n]}_{\{p\}\cup[p+2,n]}\right]^{2}\det U^{[p+3,n]}_{[p+2,n-1]}+\\
+&\det U^{[p+2,n]}_{\{p\}\cup[p+2,n-1]}\left[\det U^{[p+1,n]}_{[p+1,n]}\det U^{[q+1,n]}_{[q+1,n]}+\det U^{\{q\}\cup[q+2,n]}_{[q+1,n]}\det U^{[p+1,n]}_{\{p\}\cup[p+2,n]}\right].\end{split}

(5.12)

Applying the Desnanot-Jacobi identity (see Proposition
C.3
) to the matrix
A:=U^{[p+1,n]}_{\{p\}\cup[p+2,n]}
, we see that

\begin{split}\det U^{[p+1,n]}_{\{p\}\cup[p+2,n]}\det U^{[p+3,n]}_{[p+2,n-1]}+\det&U^{[p+2,n]}_{\{p\}\cup[p+2,n-1]}\det U^{\{p+1\}\cup[p+3,n]}_{[p+2,n]}=\\
=&\det U^{[p+2,n]}_{[p+2,n]}\det U^{\{p+1\}\cup[p+3,n]}_{\{p\}\cup[p+2,n-1]}.\end{split}

(5.13)

Now, combining the first and the third terms in (
5.12
) via (
5.13
), and then dividing both sides by
(-1)^{n-p}\det U^{[q+1,n]}_{[q+1,n]}
, formula (
5.9
) follows.

Case
q-p=1
,
q=n-1
.
In this case, the mutation at
\psi_{\square}
reads

\psi_{\square}\psi_{\square}^{\prime}=h_{n-2,n}h_{n-1,n-1}+(h_{n-1,n}^{\prime})^{2}.

(5.14)

Expanding the latter relation, we see that

\begin{split}\psi_{\square}(U)\psi_{\square}^{\prime}(U)=&u_{n-2,n}\left[\det U^{[n-1,n]}_{[n-1,n]}u_{nn}+\det U^{[n-1,n]}_{\{n-2\}\cup\{n\}}u_{n-1,n}\right]+\left[\det U^{[n-1,n]}_{\{n-2\}\cup\{n\}}\right]^{2}=\\
=&u_{nn}\left[u_{n-2,n-1}\det U^{[n-1,n]}_{\{n-2\}\cup\{n\}}+u_{n-2,n}\det U^{[n-1,n]}_{[n-1,n]}\right].\end{split}

(5.15)

Dividing both sides by
\psi_{\square}(U)=u_{nn}
, we obtain (
5.9
).

Case
q-p\neq 1
.
In this case, the mutation at
\psi_{\square}
reads

\psi_{\square}\psi_{\square}^{\prime}=h_{p+1,p+2}^{\prime}h_{qq}h_{q+1,q+2}+h_{q,q+1}h_{p+1,p+1}.

(5.16)

Expanding the right-hand side, we see that

\begin{split}(-1)^{n-q}\psi_{\square}(U)&\psi_{\square}^{\prime}(U)=\det U^{[p+1,n]}_{\{p\}\cup[p+2,n]}\det U^{[q,n]}_{[q,n]}\det U^{[q+2,n]}_{[q+1,n-1]}+\\
+&\det U^{[q+1,n]}_{[q,n-1]}\left[\det U^{[p+1,n]}_{[p+1,n]}\det U^{[q+1,n]}_{[q+1,n]}+\det U^{[p+1,n]}_{\{p\}\cup[p+2,n]}\det U^{\{q\}\cup[q+2,n]}_{[q+1,n]}\right].\end{split}

(5.17)

Applying the Desnanot-Jacobi identity (see Proposition
C.3
) to the matrix
A:=U^{[q,n]}_{[q,n]}
, we see that

\det U^{[q,n]}_{[q,n]}\det U^{[q+2,n]}_{[q+1,n-1]}+\det U^{[q+1,n]}_{[q,n-1]}\det U^{\{q\}\cup[q+2,n]}_{[q+1,n]}=\det U^{[q+1,n]}_{[q+1,n]}\det U^{\{q\}\cup[q+2,n]}_{[q,n-1]}.

(5.18)

Combining the first and the third terms in (
5.17
) with (
5.18
) and dividing both sides by
(-1)^{n-q}\psi_{\square}
, we obtain formula (
5.9
).
∎

Lemma 5.3
.

The regular functions
\psi_{\square}
and
\psi^{\prime}_{\square}
are coprime.

Proof.

Evidently,
\psi_{\square}
is irreducible, so it suffices to show that
\psi^{\prime}_{\square}(U)
is not divisible by
\psi_{\square}(U)
. If
p+1<q+1
, then
\psi_{\square}^{\prime}
is generically nonzero on the variety given by
u_{n,q+1}=u_{n,q+2}=\cdots=u_{nn}=0
, whereas
\psi_{\square}
vanishes. If
p+1>q+1
, then let
V
be the variety given by the equations

u_{i,q+1}-u_{i,q+2}=0,\ \ i\in[q+1,n].

(5.19)

We see that
\psi_{\square}(U)|_{V}=0
; on the other hand,

\psi^{\prime}_{\square}(U)|_{V}=(-1)^{\varepsilon_{\square}}\det U^{[p+1,n]}_{\{p\}\cup[p+2,n]}\det U^{\{q\}\cup[q+2,n]}_{[q,n-1]},

(5.20)

which is generically nonzero on
V
. Thus
\psi_{\square}
and
\psi_{\square}^{\prime}
are coprime.
∎

Lemma 5.4
.

Let
\mathcal{F}
be a field of characteristic zero, and let
\alpha
and
\beta
be distinct transcendental elements over
\mathcal{F}
such that
\mathcal{F}(\alpha)=\mathcal{F}(\beta)
. If there is a relation

\sum_{k=1}^{m}(\alpha^{k}-\beta^{k})p_{k}=0

(5.21)

for
p_{k}\in\mathcal{F}
, then all
p_{k}=0
.

Proof.

See
[
31
, Lemma 5.15]
.
∎

Lemma 5.5
.

For any
f\in\mathcal{O}(\GL_{n})
, if
\mathcal{Q}^{*}(f)=f
, then
f\in\mathcal{L}_{\mathbb{C}}(\Psi^{\prime})
.

Proof.

Since
\mathcal{O}(\GL_{n})\subseteq\mathcal{L}_{\mathbb{C}}(\tilde{\Psi})
, one can find a sequence of elements
f_{k}\in\mathcal{L}_{\mathbb{C}}(\tilde{\Psi})
that do not involve the variable
h_{p+1,p+1}
, such that

f(U)=\sum_{k\geq 0}f_{k}(U)[\tilde{h}_{p+1,p+1}(U)]^{k}.

(5.22)

Note that
\tilde{h}_{p+1,p+1}
is frozen in
\mathcal{GC}_{h}^{\dagger}(\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\text{std}})
, so the expression is polynomial in
\tilde{h}_{p+1,p+1}
. Applying
(\mathcal{F}^{c})^{*}
to (
5.22
) and using Lemma
5.1
together with the assumption
(\mathcal{F}^{c})^{*}(f)=f
yields

f(U)=\sum_{k\geq 0}f_{k}(U)\left[\frac{h_{p+1,p+1}(U)}{\psi_{\square}(U)}\right]^{k}.

(5.23)

Subtracting (
5.22
) from (
5.23
) and invoking Lemma
5.4
, we conclude that
f(U)=f_{0}(U)
. Since
\tilde{\psi}_{\square}(U)
is frozen in
\mathcal{GC}_{h}^{\dagger}(\tilde{\mathbf{\Gamma}})
, it enters
f_{0}(U)
only with nonnegative exponents, so one can rewrite it in terms of a fraction with
\psi_{\square}^{\prime}
in the denominator. Thus
f\in\mathcal{L}_{\mathbb{C}}(\Psi^{\prime})
.
∎

Proposition 5.6
.

Under the setup of the current subsection, the following statements hold:

1)

All the variables in
\Psi
are regular and irreducible;

2)

For any cluster variable
\psi\in\Psi
, its mutation
\psi^{\prime}
is regular and coprime with
\psi
;

3)

The generalized cluster structures
\mathcal{GC}_{h}^{\dagger}(\mathbf{\Gamma},\GL_{n})
and
\mathcal{GC}_{h}^{\dagger}(\mathbf{\Gamma},\SL_{n})
are complete.

Proof.

By Lemma
5.3
, the variable
\psi_{\square}^{\prime}
is regular and coprime with
\psi_{\square}
in the ring
\mathcal{O}(\GL_{n})
. By Proposition
2.28
, it remains to show that
\mathcal{O}(\GL_{n})\subseteq\mathcal{L}_{\mathbb{C}}(\Psi^{\prime})
. Since
\mathcal{O}(\GL_{n})\subseteq\mathcal{L}_{\mathbb{C}}(\tilde{\Psi})
, it suffices to show that
\mathcal{L}_{\mathbb{C}}(\tilde{\Psi})\subseteq\mathcal{L}_{\mathbb{C}}(\Psi^{\prime})
; in turn, due to Lemma
5.1
, proving the latter inclusion reduces to showing that
\det U^{[p+1,n]}_{[p+1,n]}\in\mathcal{L}_{\mathbb{C}}(\Psi^{\prime})
. First, let us notice that

(\mathcal{F}^{c})^{*}\left(\det U^{\{q\}\cup[q+2,n]}_{I_{\square}}\right)=\det U^{\{q\}\cup[q+2,n]}_{I_{\square}},

(5.24)

where
I_{\square}
is given by (
5.10
). By Lemma
5.5
,
\det U^{\{q\}\cup[q+2,n]}_{I_{\square}}\in\mathcal{L}_{\mathbb{C}}(\Psi^{\prime})
. Second, notice that
\det U^{[q+1,n]}_{I_{\square}}
is a cluster variable in
\Psi^{\prime}
(hence an invertible element of
\mathcal{L}_{\mathbb{C}}(\Psi^{\prime})
), for

\det U^{[q+1,n]}_{I_{\square}}=\begin{cases}(-1)^{n-q}h_{q,q+1}(U)\ &\text{if}\ q-p\neq 1;\\
(-1)^{n-p}h^{\prime}_{p+1,p+3}(U)\ &\text{if}\ q-p=1,\ p\leq n-3;\\
(-1)^{n-p}h_{pn}(U)\ &\text{if}\ q-p=1,\ p=n-2.\end{cases}

(5.25)

It follows that one can solve the equation (
5.9
) for
\det U^{[p+1,n]}_{[p+1,n]}
in the ring
\mathcal{L}_{\mathbb{C}}(\Psi^{\prime})
. Due to the discussion in the beginning of the proof, we finally conclude that the statement holds.
∎

5.1.2
The case of
|\Gamma_{1}|=|\Gamma_{2}|=1
(initial cluster).

Although in the previous subsection we proved
\bar{\mathcal{A}}(\mathcal{GC}_{h}^{\dagger}(\mathbf{\Gamma},\GL_{n}))=\mathcal{O}(\GL_{n})
for
\mathbf{\Gamma}=(\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2},\gamma)
with
|\Gamma_{1}|=1
, we proved regularity, irreducibility and coprimality with mutations not in the initial extended cluster
\Psi_{0}
, but in a certain shifted extended cluster
\Psi
. According to Proposition
2.28
, in order to claim the same properties for
\Psi_{0}
, we need to verify them for the marked variable in
\Psi_{0}
. As above, let us assume that
\Gamma_{1}=\{p\}
and
\Gamma_{2}=\{q\}
, and set
\psi_{\square}:=h_{q+1,q+1}
.

Lemma 5.7
.

The mutation of
\Psi_{0}
at the marked variable
\psi_{\square}
produces the variable

\psi_{\square}^{\prime}(U)=\begin{cases}(-1)^{p+q+1}\left[u_{p+1,n}\det U^{[q+1,n]}_{[q,n-1]}+u_{pn}\det U^{\{q\}\cup[q+2,n]}_{[q,n-1]}\right]\ &q\neq p+1;\\[10.0pt]
\det U^{[q+1,n]}_{[q+1,n]}\left[u_{p+1,n}\det U^{[q+1,n]}_{[q,n-1]}+u_{pn}\det U^{\{q\}\cup[q+2,n]}_{[q,n-1]}\right]+\\[7.0pt]
+\det U^{\{q\}\cup[q+2,n]}_{[q+1,n]}\left[u_{p+1,n}\det U^{[q+1,n]}_{\{q-1\}\cup[q+1,n-1]}+u_{pn}\det U^{\{q\}\cup[q+2,n]}_{\{q-1\}\cup[q+1,n-1]}\right]\ &q=p+1.\end{cases}

Proof.

The exchange relation is given by

\psi_{\square}\psi_{\square}^{\prime}=h_{q,q+1}h_{p+1,n}+h_{qq}h_{pn}h_{q+1,q+2}.

(5.26)

Let us study two cases:

Case
q\neq p+1
.
Writing out all the functions, the relation becomes

\begin{split}(-1)^{p+q+1}\psi_{\square}(U)\psi_{\square}^{\prime}(U)=\det&U^{[q+1,n]}_{[q,n-1]}u_{p+1,n}\det U^{[q+1,n]}_{[q+1,n]}+\\
+&u_{pn}\left(\det U^{[q+1,n]}_{[q,n-1]}\det U^{\{q\}\cup[q+2,n]}_{[q+1,n]}+\det U^{[q,n]}_{[q,n]}\det U^{[q+2,n]}_{[q+1,n-1]}\right).\end{split}

(5.27)

Applying the Desnanot-Jacobi relation (see Proposition
C.3
) to the last term, we see that

\det U^{[q+1,n]}_{[q,n-1]}\det U^{\{q\}\cup[q+2,n]}_{[q+1,n]}+\det U^{[q,n]}_{[q,n]}\det U^{[q+2,n]}_{[q+1,n-1]}=\det U^{[q+1,n]}_{[q+1,n]}\det U^{\{q\}\cup[q+2,n]}_{[q,n-1]};

hence, after dividing both sides by
\det U^{[q+1,n]}_{[q+1,n]}
in (
5.27
), the result follows.

Case
q=p+1
.
Writing out all functions and rearranging the terms, the relation becomes

\begin{split}\psi_{\square}^{\prime}(U)=u_{p+1,n}&\left[\det U^{[q+1,n]}_{[q+1,n]}\det U^{[q+1,n]}_{[q,n-1]}+\det U^{[q+1,n]}_{\{q-1\}\cup[q+1,n-1]}\det U^{\{q\}\cup[q+2,n]}_{[q+1,n]}\right]+\\
+u_{pn}&\left[\det U^{[q+1,n]}_{[q,n-1]}\det U^{\{q\}\cup[q+2,n]}_{[q+1,n]}+\det U^{[q,n]}_{[q,n]}\det U^{[q+2,n]}_{[q+1,n-1]}\right]+\\
+&\frac{u_{pn}\det U^{\{q\}\cup[q+2,n]}_{[q+1,n]}}{\det U^{[q+1,n]}_{[q+1,n]}}\cdot A\end{split}

(5.28)

where
A
is given by

A=\det U^{[q+1,n]}_{\{q-1\}\cup[q+1,n-1]}\det U^{\{q\}\cup[q+2,n]}_{[q+1,n]}+\det U^{[q,n]}_{\{q-1\}\cup[q+1,n]}\det U^{[q+2,n]}_{[q+1,n-1]}.

(5.29)

The second term in (
5.28
) can be simplified via the Desnanot-Jacobi identity as

\det U^{[q+1,n]}_{[q,n-1]}\det U^{\{q\}\cup[q+2,n]}_{[q+1,n]}+\det U^{[q,n]}_{[q,n]}\det U^{[q+2,n]}_{[q+1,n-1]}=\det U^{[q+1,n]}_{[q+1,n]}\det U^{\{q\}\cup[q+2,n]}_{[q,n-1]};

(5.30)

likewise, formula (
5.29
) can be simplified as

\begin{split}\det U^{[q+1,n]}_{\{q-1\}\cup[q+1,n-1]}\det U^{\{q\}\cup[q+2,n]}_{[q+1,n]}+\det&U^{[q,n]}_{\{q-1\}\cup[q+1,n]}\det U^{[q+2,n]}_{[q+1,n-1]}=\\
=&\det U^{[q+1,n]}_{[q+1,n]}\det U^{\{q\}\cup[q+2,n]}_{\{q-1\}\cup[q+1,n-1]}.\end{split}

(5.31)

Combining the above terms, the formula for
\psi_{\square}^{\prime}
follows.
∎

Proposition 5.8
.

Under the setup of the current subsection, the following statements hold:

1)

All the variables in
\Psi_{0}
are regular and irreducible;

2)

For any cluster variable
\psi\in\Psi_{0}
, its mutation
\psi^{\prime}
is regular and coprime with
\psi
.

Proof.

According to Proposition
2.28
, it is enough to verify the above statements only for
\psi_{\square}
. Evidently,
\psi_{\square}
is irreducible and
\psi_{\square}^{\prime}
is regular (see Lemma
5.7
). If
q\neq p+1
, we see that
\psi_{\square}
vanishes on the variety given by
u_{n,q+1}=u_{n,q+2}=\cdots=u_{nn}=0
, whereas
\psi_{\square}^{\prime}(U)
is generically nonzero. If
q=p+1
, then it suffices to check that

u_{p+1,n}\det U^{[q+1,n]}_{\{q-1\}\cup[q+1,n-1]}+u_{pn}\det U^{\{q\}\cup[q+2,n]}_{\{q-1\}\cup[q+1,n-1]}

(5.32)

is not divisible by
\psi_{\square}(U)
, which immediately follows from the same argument.
∎

5.1.3
Completeness for any BD triple

In this subsection, we finalize the proofs of Part
2
of Theorem
1.5
and Part
2
of Theorem
1.6
.

Proposition 5.9
.

Let
\mathbf{\Gamma}:=(\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2},\gamma)
be a BD triple of type
A_{n-1}
and
G\in\{\SL_{n},\GL_{n}\}
. Then the following statements hold:

1)

The generalized cluster structure
\mathcal{GC}_{h}^{\dagger}(\mathbf{\Gamma},G)
is regular;

2)

All the variables in the initial extended cluster of
\mathcal{GC}_{h}^{\dagger}(\mathbf{\Gamma},G)
are irreducible (as elements of
\mathcal{O}(G)
) and (for cluster variables) coprime with their mutations;

3)

The generalized cluster structure
\mathcal{GC}_{h}^{\dagger}(\mathbf{\Gamma},G)
is complete.

Proof.

The regularity of the initial extended cluster follows from Proposition
6.4
. As in the previous papers
[
23
,
31
]
, we run an induction on the size
|\Gamma_{1}|
for a fixed
n
. For
|\Gamma_{1}|=0
, the statements were proved in
[
20
, Theorem 3.8, Theorem 3.10]
; for
|\Gamma_{1}|=1
, the statements are contained in Proposition
5.6
and Proposition
5.8
. For
|\Gamma_{1}|>1
, the statements follow from the existence of complementary birational quasi-isomorphisms
\mathcal{G}
and Proposition
2.30
.
∎

5.2
Toric action

In this subsection, we prove Part
3
of Theorems
1.5
and
1.6
. Given a BD triple
\mathbf{\Gamma}:=(\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2},\gamma)
of type
A_{n-1}
, let us recall that

\mathfrak{h}_{\mathbf{\Gamma}}=\{h\in\mathfrak{h}^{s}\ |\ \alpha(h)=\beta(h)\ \text{if}\ \gamma^{j}(\alpha)=\beta\ \text{for some}\ j\}.

(5.33)

where
\mathfrak{h}^{s}
is the Cartan subalgebra of
\sll_{n}(\mathbb{C})
(relative the a priori fixed root system). The dimension of
\mathfrak{h}_{\mathbf{\Gamma}}
is equal to
k_{\mathbf{\Gamma}}:=|\Pi\setminus\Gamma_{1}|
. Let
\mathcal{H}_{\mathbf{\Gamma}}
be the connected subgroup of
\SL_{n}
with Lie algebra
\mathfrak{h}_{\mathbf{\Gamma}}
. We let the group
\mathcal{H}_{\mathbf{\Gamma}}
act upon
\SL_{n}
by conjugation.

Lemma 5.10
.

Let
\Gamma_{1}=\{p\}
and
\Gamma_{2}=\{q\}
for some simple roots
p
and
q
. Let
\psi_{\square}:=h_{q+1,q+1}
be the marked variable. Then the
y
-variable
y(\psi_{\square})
is invariant with respect to the action by
\mathcal{H}_{\mathbf{\Gamma}}
.

Proof.

Let us recall from Proposition
4.16
that
\mathcal{Q}
is equivariant with respect to the action by
\mathcal{H}_{\mathbf{\Gamma}}
. In the setup of the lemma,
\mathcal{H}_{\mathbf{\Gamma}}
is given by

\mathcal{H}_{\mathbf{\Gamma}}=\{T=\diag(t_{1},t_{2},\ldots,t_{n})\ |\ \det T=1,\ t_{p}t_{p+1}^{-1}t_{q+1}t_{q}^{-1}=1\}.

(5.34)

Applying
\mathcal{Q}^{*}
to
y(\psi_{\square})
, we see that

\mathcal{Q}^{*}(y(\psi_{\square}))=\frac{\tilde{h}_{qq}}{\tilde{h}_{q,q+1}}\frac{\tilde{h}_{pn}}{\tilde{h}_{p+1,n}}\frac{\tilde{h}_{q+1,q+2}}{\tilde{h}_{q+1,q+1}}.

(5.35)

Now, it is a simple observation that
\tilde{h}_{q+1,q+1}(U)
and
\tilde{h}_{qq}(U)
are invariant with respect to
\mathcal{H}_{\mathbf{\Gamma}}
, as well as

\displaystyle\tilde{h}_{pn}(TUT^{-1})=t_{p}t_{n}^{-1}\tilde{h}_{pn}(U),\

\displaystyle\tilde{h}_{q+1,q+2}(TUT^{-1})=t_{q+1}t_{n}^{-1}\tilde{h}_{q+1,q+2}(U),

(5.36)

\displaystyle\tilde{h}_{q,q+1}(TUT^{-1})=t_{q}t_{n}^{-1}\tilde{h}(U),\

\displaystyle\tilde{h}_{p+1,n}(TUT^{-1})=t_{p+1}t_{n}^{-1}\tilde{h}_{p+1,n}(U).

Therefore,

\mathcal{Q}^{*}\left(y(\psi_{\square})(TUT^{-1})\right)=\mathcal{Q}^{*}\left(y(\psi_{\square})(U)\right)\frac{t_{p}t_{n}^{-1}t_{q+1}t_{n}^{-1}}{t_{q}t_{n}^{-1}t_{p+1}t_{n}^{-1}}=\mathcal{Q}^{*}\left(y(\psi_{\square})(U)\right).

(5.37)

It follows that
y(\psi_{\square})
is invariant with respect to the action by
\mathcal{H}_{\mathbf{\Gamma}}
.
∎

Proposition 5.11
.

Let
\mathbf{\Gamma}
be a BD triple of type
A_{n-1}
. The following statements hold:

1)

The action of
\mathcal{H}_{\mathbf{\Gamma}}
upon
\SL_{n}
induces a global toric action upon
\mathcal{GC}_{h}^{\dagger}(\mathbf{\Gamma},\SL_{n})
of rank
k_{\mathbf{\Gamma}}
;

2)

The action of
\mathcal{H}_{\mathbf{\Gamma}}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}
upon
\GL_{n}
induces a global toric action upon
\mathcal{GC}_{h}^{\dagger}(\mathbf{\Gamma},\GL_{n})
of rank
k_{\mathbf{\Gamma}}+1
.

Proof.

The statement for
\mathbb{C}^{*}
was already proved in Lemma
4.21
, so let us focus on
\mathcal{H}_{\mathbf{\Gamma}}
; the proof is based on Proposition
2.19
. Let us notice that due to Proposition
4.16
, all birational quasi-isomorphisms
\mathcal{G}
that were introduced in Sections
4.4
and
4.5
are equivariant with respect to
\mathcal{H}_{\mathbf{\Gamma}}
. This implies that
\mathcal{H}_{\mathbf{\Gamma}}
induces a local toric action upon the initial extended cluster of
\mathcal{GC}_{h}^{\dagger}(\mathbf{\Gamma})
. The fact that the action is of maximal rank follows from a standard argument
18
18

18

Let us briefly recall the argument. Assume that the toric action is of rank less than maximal. This means that there exists a
1
-dimensional subgroup of
\mathcal{H}_{\mathbf{\Gamma}}
that leaves all cluster and frozen variables from the initial extended cluster
\Psi_{0}
invariant. However, since
\mathcal{O}(\GL_{n})\subseteq\mathcal{L}_{\mathbb{C}}(\Psi_{0})
, we see that the action leaves
all
regular functions invariant. In other words, it is trivial.
that was utilized in
[
23
,
31
]
. The invariance of the Casimirs
\hat{p}_{ir}
(which are the
c
-variables in our case) was proved before in
[
20
, Theorem 6.1]
; therefore, it remains to show that all the
y
-variables in the initial extended cluster
\Psi_{0}
of
\mathcal{GC}_{h}^{\dagger}(\mathbf{\Gamma})
are invariant with respect to the action by
\mathcal{H}_{\mathbf{\Gamma}}
.

We proceed by induction on the size
|\Gamma_{1}|
. If
|\Gamma_{1}|=0
, then the statement was already proved in
[
20
, Theorem 6.1]
. Let
\mathbf{\Gamma}
be a BD triple of type
A_{n-1}
and
\tilde{\mathbf{\Gamma}}
be obtained from
\mathbf{\Gamma}
via removal of a pair of roots. Let
\psi_{\square}
be the corresponding marked variable. Notice that
\mathfrak{h}_{\tilde{\mathbf{\Gamma}}}\supset\mathfrak{h}_{\mathbf{\Gamma}}
. If
|\Gamma_{1}|=1
and
y(\psi)
is a
y
-variable of
\psi\neq\psi_{\square}
, we see that

\mathcal{G}^{*}\left(y(\psi)(TUT^{-1})\right)=y(\psi)(T\mathcal{G}(U)T^{-1})=y(\psi)(\mathcal{G}(TUT^{-1}))=y(\tilde{\psi})(U).

(5.38)

If
\psi=\psi_{\square}
, then the invariance follows from Lemma
5.10
. The case of
|\Gamma_{1}|>1
follows from the existence of complementary birational quasi-isomorphisms
\mathcal{G}
(see Section
4.4
).
∎

5.3
Compatibility

In this subsection, we use the Poisson properties of the birational quasi-isomorphism
\mathcal{Q}
in order to prove Part
4
of Theorem
1.5
and Theorem
1.6
; that is, the generalized cluster structure
\mathcal{GC}_{h}^{\dagger}(\mathbf{\Gamma})
is compatible with
\pi^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{\Gamma}}
for any BD triple
\mathbf{\Gamma}
of type
A_{n-1}
.

Lemma 5.12
.

Let
\mathbf{\Gamma}:=(\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2},\gamma)
be a BD triple such that
\Gamma_{1}=\{p\}
,
\Gamma_{2}=\{q\}
for some simple roots
p
and
q
. Then for any cluster or frozen variable
\psi
from the initial extended cluster of
\mathcal{GC}^{\dagger}(\mathbf{\Gamma})
,

\{\log y(\psi_{\square}),\log\psi\}=\begin{cases}1\ &\text{if}\ \psi=h_{q+1,q+1},\\
0\ &\text{otherwise}\end{cases}

(5.39)

where
\psi_{\square}:=h_{q+1,q+1}
is the marked variable.

Proof.

Let us apply the birational quasi-isomorphism
\mathcal{Q}
and prove an equivalent statement for the trivial BD triple. We see that

\mathcal{Q}^{*}(y(\psi_{\square}))=\frac{\tilde{h}_{qq}}{\tilde{h}_{q,q+1}}\frac{\tilde{h}_{pn}}{\tilde{h}_{p+1,n}}\frac{\tilde{h}_{q+1,q+2}}{\tilde{h}_{q+1,q+1}}.

(5.40)

Let us first consider the case when
\psi
is an
h
-variable. If
h_{1}
and
h_{2}
are two functions on
\GL_{n}
such that
h_{i}(UN_{-})=h_{i}(U)
, where
N_{-}
is a unipotent lower triangular matrix and
i\in\{1,2\}
, then the Poisson bracket
\{\cdot,\cdot\}_{\text{std}}^{\dagger}
between them is given by

\{h_{1},h_{2}\}_{\text{std}}^{\dagger}=\langle\pi_{>}\nabla_{U}^{R}h_{1},\nabla_{U}^{R}h_{2}\rangle+\langle R_{0}[\nabla_{U}h_{1},U],[\nabla_{U}h_{2},U]\rangle-\langle\pi_{0}[\nabla_{U}h_{1},U],\nabla_{U}h_{2}U\rangle.

(5.41)

It follows from Lemma
C.4
that

\langle\pi_{>}\left(\nabla_{U}^{R}\log\frac{\tilde{h}_{pn}}{\tilde{h}_{p+1,n}}\right),\nabla_{U}^{R}\log\tilde{h}_{ks}\rangle=-\chi_{n-s=p-k}

(5.42)

\langle\pi_{>}\left(\nabla_{U}^{R}\log\frac{\tilde{h}_{qq}}{\tilde{h}_{q,q+1}}\frac{\tilde{h}_{q+1,q+2}}{\tilde{h}_{q+1,q+1}}\right),\nabla_{U}^{R}\log\tilde{h}_{ks}\rangle=-\chi_{k\leq q}\cdot\chi_{s=q+1}

(5.43)

where
\chi_{(\cdot)}\in\{0,1\}
is an indicator that returns true or false depending on whether the corresponding condition is satisfied. It follows from Lemma
C.5
that

\pi_{0}\left(\nabla_{U}^{L}\log\mathcal{Q}^{*}y(\psi_{\square})\right)=e_{qq}-e_{q+1,q+1},\ \ \pi_{0}\left(\nabla_{U}^{R}\mathcal{Q}^{*}y(\psi_{\square})\right)=e_{pp}-e_{p+1,p+1};

(5.44)

hence,

\begin{split}R_{0}[\nabla_{U}\log\mathcal{Q}^{*}&y(\psi_{\square}),U]=R_{0}(e_{qq}-e_{q+1,q+1}-(e_{pp}-e_{p+1,p+1}))=\\
=&-R_{0}(1-\gamma)(e_{pp}-e_{p+1,p+1})=-e_{pp}+e_{p+1,p+1}.\end{split}

(5.45)

Let us set
\Delta(i,j):=\sum_{k=i}^{j}e_{kk}
. We see that

\begin{split}\{\log\mathcal{Q}^{*}y(\psi_{\square}),\log\hat{h}_{ks}\}_{\text{std}}^{\dagger}=&-\chi_{n-s=p-k}-\chi_{k\leq q}\cdot\chi_{s=q+1}+\\
+&\langle e_{pp}-e_{p+1,p+1},\Delta(k,n-s+k)\rangle-\langle e_{qq}-e_{q+1,q+1},\Delta(s,n)\rangle=\\
=&-\chi_{k=p+1}+(1-\chi_{k\leq q})\chi_{s=q+1}=-\chi_{k=p+1}+\chi_{k=q+1}\chi_{s=q+1}.\end{split}

(5.46)

Now let us recall that
\mathcal{Q}^{*}({h_{ks}})=\tilde{h}_{ks}
if
k\neq p+1
and
\mathcal{Q}^{*}(h_{p+1,s})=\tilde{h}_{p+1,s}\tilde{h}_{q+1,q+1}
. Together with formula (
5.46
), the result follows in the case
\psi
is an
h
-function. If
\psi=\varphi_{kl}
for some
\varphi
-function
\varphi_{kl}
, then for any regular function
h
such that
h(UN_{-})=h(U)
,

\{\log h,\log\varphi_{kl}\}_{\text{std}}^{\dagger}=\langle R_{0}\pi_{0}[\nabla_{U}\log h,U],[\nabla_{U}\log\varphi_{kl},U]\rangle-\langle\pi_{0}\nabla_{U}^{R}\log h,[\nabla_{U}\log\varphi_{kl},U]\rangle.

(5.47)

Setting
h:=\log\mathcal{Q}^{*}y(\psi_{\square})
and applying
\pi_{0}[\nabla_{U}\log\varphi_{kl},U]=\mathrm{const}
together with (
5.44
) and (
5.45
), we obtain that

\{\log\mathcal{Q}^{*}y(\psi_{\square}),\log\varphi_{kl}\}_{\text{std}}^{\dagger}=0.

(5.48)

Thus the result follows.
∎

Proposition 5.13
.

Let
\mathbf{\Gamma}
be a BD triple of type
A_{n-1}
. For any choice of
r_{0}
relative the triple
\mathbf{\Gamma}
, the generalized cluster structure
\mathcal{GC}_{h}^{\dagger}(\mathbf{\Gamma})
is compatible with
\pi_{\mathbf{\Gamma}}^{\dagger}
.

Proof.

Let us fix
n
and run an induction on the size
|\Gamma_{1}|
. For
|\Gamma_{1}|=0
, the result was proved in
[
20
, Theorem 3.8]
. Let
\mathbf{\Gamma}
be any BD triple of type
A_{n-1}
and
\tilde{\mathbf{\Gamma}}
be the BD triple obtained from
\mathbf{\Gamma}
via removal of
p\in\Gamma_{1}
and
q\in\Gamma_{2}
. Let
\mathcal{G}:(G,\pi_{\tilde{\mathbf{\Gamma}}}^{\dagger})\dashrightarrow(G,\pi_{\mathbf{\Gamma}}^{\dagger})
be the birational quasi-isomorphism constructed in Section
4.5
, where
G\in\{\SL_{n},\GL_{n}\}
. Let
\tilde{\Psi}_{0}
and
\Psi_{0}
be the initial extended clusters of
\mathcal{GC}_{h}^{\dagger}(\tilde{\mathbf{\Gamma}},G)
and
\mathcal{GC}_{h}^{\dagger}(\mathbf{\Gamma},G)
, and let
\psi_{\square}\in\Psi_{0}
be the marked variable. Since
\tilde{\mathbf{\Gamma}}\prec\mathbf{\Gamma}
, any
r_{0}
component for
\pi_{\mathbf{\Gamma}}^{\dagger}
is also an
r_{0}
component for
\pi_{\tilde{\mathbf{\Gamma}}}^{\dagger}
; hence, we can choose the same
r_{0}
to make
\mathcal{G}
a Poisson isomorphism (the result of Proposition
4.17
). Now, since
\tilde{\Psi}_{0}
is assumed to be log-canonical and
\mathcal{G}
is a Poisson isomorphism,
\Psi_{0}
is log-canonical as well. Let
\psi_{1}\in\Psi_{0}\setminus\{\psi_{\square}\}
be a cluster variable and
\psi_{2}\in\Psi_{0}
be any cluster or frozen variable. Since

\{\log y(\tilde{\psi}_{1}),\log\tilde{\psi}_{2}\}=\chi_{\tilde{\psi}_{1}=\tilde{\psi}_{2}},

(5.49)

an application of
(\mathcal{G}^{*})^{-1}
yields

\{\log y(\psi_{1}),\log\psi_{2}\}=\chi_{\psi_{1}=\psi_{2}}.

(5.50)

If
|\Gamma_{1}|=1
, it follows from Lemma
5.12
(which treats the case of
\psi_{1}=\psi_{\square}
) and Proposition
2.16
that
\mathcal{GC}_{h}^{\dagger}(\mathbf{\Gamma},G)
is compatible with
\pi_{\mathbf{\Gamma}}^{\dagger}
. For
|\Gamma_{1}|>1
, to verify (
5.50
) for the marked variables, one can use another birational quasi-isomorphism
\mathcal{G}
whose marked variables are disjoint from the former one. Thus the statement of the proposition holds.
∎

6
Further properties of
h
-variables

In this section, we provide several additional formulas for the
h
-variables, including their explicit expansion in terms of minors of an element
U\in\GL_{n}
. Also, we study the Poisson properties of the frozen
h
-variables and prove Proposition
1.8
.

6.1
Preliminaries

In this subsection, we derive preliminary formulas that we will need in order to compute the
h
-variables explicitly in terms of minors of
U
.

Notation.

Let
A\subseteq[1,n]
,
i\in[1,n]
. Set
A^{\leq}(i):=A\cap[1,i]
,
A^{\geq}(i):=A\cap[i,n]
, and for a number
t\in[1,|A|]
denote by
{A\choose t}
the set of all subsets of
A
of size
t
.

Lemma 6.1
.

Let
\mathbf{\Gamma}:=(\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2},\gamma)
be a BD triple of type
A_{n-1}
. Let
i\in[1,n]
,
r\in[i,n]
,
k\geq 0
and
J\subseteq[1,n]
,
|J|=r-i+1
. Let
\Delta
be the
X
-run that contains
i
. For
i-1\in\Gamma_{1}
, set

L_{i,r}:=\begin{cases}[\bar{\gamma}(i)_{+}(\Gamma_{2})+1,n]\ &\text{if}\ r\notin\Delta\ \text{or}\ \gamma|_{\Delta\cap\Gamma_{2}}<0;\\
[\bar{\gamma}(r)+1,n]\ &\text{if}\ r\in\Delta\ \text{and}\ \gamma|_{\Delta\cap\Gamma_{1}}>0;\end{cases}

(6.1)

D_{i,I}(U):=\begin{cases}\dfrac{\det U^{\check{\gamma}(I)\cup L_{i,r}}_{[\bar{\gamma}(i),n]}}{\det U^{[\bar{\gamma}(i),n]}_{[\bar{\gamma}(i),n]}}\ &\text{if}\ \gamma|_{\Delta\cap\Gamma_{1}}>0;\\[20.0pt]
\dfrac{\det U^{\check{\gamma}(I)\cup L_{i,r}}_{[\bar{\gamma}(i)+1,n]}}{\det U^{[\bar{\gamma}(i)+1,n]}_{[\bar{\gamma}(i)+1,n]}}\ &\text{if}\ \gamma|_{\Delta\cap\Gamma_{1}}<0.\end{cases}

(6.2)

Then for any
k\geq 0
,

\det[\mathcal{F}_{k}(U)]_{[i,r]}^{J}=\begin{cases}\sum_{I\in{\Delta^{\leq}(r)\choose|\Delta\cap[i,r]|}}\mathcal{D}_{i,I}(\mathcal{F}_{k-1}(U))\cdot\det U^{J}_{I\cup[i_{+}(\Gamma_{1})+1,r]}\ &\text{if}\ i-1\in\Gamma_{1},\\[12.0pt]
\det U_{[i,r]}^{J}\ &\text{otherwise.}\end{cases}

(6.3)

Proof.

By formula (
4.9
) (the definition of
\mathcal{F}_{k}(U)
) and the Cauchy-Binet formula,

\det[\mathcal{F}_{k}(U)]^{K}_{[i,r]}=\sum_{K,\ |K|=|i,r|}\det[\tilde{\gamma}^{*}(\mathcal{F}_{k-1}(U)_{-})]_{[i,r]}^{K}\det U^{J}_{K}.

(6.4)

Let us notice that the shape of the matrix
[\tilde{\gamma}^{*}(U_{-})]_{[i,n]}^{[1,n]}
is given by

[\tilde{\gamma}^{*}(U_{-})]_{[i,n]}^{[1,n]}=\begin{bmatrix}0&[\tilde{\gamma}^{*}(U_{-})]_{[i,i_{+}(\Gamma_{1})]}^{\Delta}&0\\
0&0&[\tilde{\gamma}^{*}(U_{-})]_{[i_{+}(\Gamma_{1})+1,n]}^{[i_{+}(\Gamma_{1})+1,n]}\end{bmatrix};

(6.5)

therefore,
\det[\tilde{\gamma}^{*}(U_{-})]_{[i,r]}^{K}
is nontrivial for a generic
U\in\GL_{n}
if and only if
K=I\cup[i_{+}(\Gamma_{1})+1,r]
where
I\in{\Delta^{\leq}(r)\choose|\Delta\cap[i,r]|}
. Hence
\det[\tilde{\gamma}^{*}(U_{-})]^{K}_{[i,r]}=\det[\tilde{\gamma}^{*}(U_{-})]^{I}_{\Delta\cap[i,r]}
. Now, formula (
6.2
) follows from formulas (
C.2
)–(
C.3
) and the definitions of
\bar{\gamma}
and
\check{\gamma}
given in Section
4.1
.
∎

Lemma 6.2
.

Let
\mathbf{\Gamma}=(\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2},\gamma)
be a BD triple of type
A_{n-1}
and
S^{\gamma}(\alpha_{0}):=\{\alpha_{i}\}_{i\geq 0}^{m}
be a
\gamma
-string. Then for any
i\in[0,m]
,
r\geq\alpha_{m-i}+1
,
J\subseteq[1,n]
,
|J|=|[\alpha_{m-i}+1,r]|
, and any
\ell\geq 0
,

\det[\mathcal{F}(U)]_{[\alpha_{m-i}+1,r]}^{J}=\det[\mathcal{F}_{i+\ell}(U)]_{[\alpha_{m-i}+1,r]}^{J}.

(6.6)

Proof.

Let us prove the statement by induction on
i
. For
i=0
, the statement is contained in the second line of formula (
6.3
), so let us assume that the statement holds for
i\geq 0
and prove it for
i+1
. For each
t\in[0,m]
, denote by
\Delta_{t}
the
X
-run that contains
\alpha_{t}
. Notice that

\displaystyle\bar{\gamma}(\alpha_{t}+1)

\displaystyle=\alpha_{t+1}+1\

\displaystyle\text{if}\ \gamma|_{\Delta_{i}\cap\Gamma_{1}}>0,

(6.7)

\displaystyle\bar{\gamma}(\alpha_{t}+1)+1

\displaystyle=\alpha_{t+1}+1\

\displaystyle\text{if}\ \gamma|_{\Delta_{i}\cap\Gamma_{1}}<0.

For
I\in{\Delta_{m-i-1}^{\leq}(r)\choose|\Delta_{i}\cap[\alpha_{m-i-1},r]}
, the latter formulas together with formula (
6.2
) imply that

D_{\alpha_{m-i-1}+1,I}(U)=\frac{\det U^{\check{\gamma}(I)\cup L_{\alpha_{m-i-1}+1,r}}_{[\alpha_{m-i}+1,n]}}{\det U^{[\alpha_{m-i}+1,n]}_{[\alpha_{m-i}+1,n]}}

(6.8)

regardless of the orientation of
\gamma
on
\Delta_{m-i-1}
. Now, it follows from formula (
6.3
), the assumption of the induction and formula (
6.8
) that

\begin{split}\det[\mathcal{F}_{i+1+\ell}(U)]^{J}_{[\alpha_{m-i-1}+1,r]}=&\sum_{I\in{\Delta_{m-i-1}^{\leq}(r)\choose|\Delta_{m-i-1}\cap[\alpha_{m-i-1}+1,r]}}\hskip-10.0ptD_{\alpha_{m-i-1}+1,I}(\mathcal{F}_{i+\ell}(U))\det U^{J}_{I\cup[(\alpha_{m-i-1})_{+}(\Gamma_{1})+1,r]}=\\
=&\sum_{I\in{\Delta_{m-i-1}^{\leq}(r)\choose|\Delta_{m-i-1}\cap[\alpha_{m-i-1}+1,r]}}\hskip-10.0ptD_{\alpha_{m-i-1}+1,I}(\mathcal{F}(U))\det U^{J}_{I\cup[(\alpha_{m-i-1})_{+}(\Gamma_{1})+1,r]}=\\
=&\det[\mathcal{F}(U)]_{[\alpha_{m-i-1}+1,r]}^{J}.\end{split}

Thus the statement holds.
∎

As a consequence of Lemma
6.2
, the defining formula for the
h
-variables (
1.11
) can be recast in the following way:

Proposition 6.3
.

Let
\mathbf{\Gamma}:=(\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2},\gamma)
be a BD triple of type
A_{n-1}
and
S^{\gamma}(\alpha_{0}):=\{\alpha_{i}\}_{i=0}^{m}
be a
\gamma
-string. For any
i\in[0,m]
and
j\in[\alpha_{i}+1,n]
, the
h
-variable
h_{\alpha_{i}+1,j}(U)
can be written as

h_{\alpha_{i}+1,j}(U)=(-1)^{\varepsilon_{\alpha_{i}+1,j}}\det[\mathcal{F}_{m-i}(U)]^{[j,n]}_{[\alpha_{i}+1,n-j+\alpha_{i}+1]}\prod_{t\geq i+1}^{m}\det[\mathcal{F}_{m-t}(U)]^{[\alpha_{t}+1,n]}_{[\alpha_{t}+1,n]}

(6.9)

where
\varepsilon_{\alpha_{i}+1,j}\in\{0,1\}
is given by formula (
1.12
).

Proof.

The statement is a straightforward consequence of Lemma
6.2
.
∎

6.2
Explicit expansion of the
h
-variables

In this subsection, we derive an explicit formula for the
h
-variables in terms of minors of
U\in\GL_{n}
. Let us fix a BD triple
\mathbf{\Gamma}:=(\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2},\gamma)
and a
\gamma
-string
S^{\gamma}(\alpha_{0}):=\{\alpha_{i}\}_{i=0}^{m}
. Define the following data:

\displaystyle\Delta_{i}

\displaystyle:=\Delta(\alpha_{i})=\text{the }X\text{-run that contains }\alpha_{i},

\displaystyle 0\leq i\leq m;

(6.10)

\displaystyle s_{ij}

\displaystyle:=n-j+\alpha_{i}+1,

\displaystyle i\in[0,m],\ j\in[\alpha_{i}+1,n];

\displaystyle K_{i}

\displaystyle:=[(\alpha_{i})_{+}(\Gamma_{1})+1,n],

\displaystyle i\in[0,m-1];

\displaystyle L_{i}

\displaystyle:=[(\alpha_{i})_{+}(\Gamma_{2})+1,n],

\displaystyle i\in[2,m];

\displaystyle L^{\prime}_{i}(j)

\displaystyle:=\begin{cases}[\max\check{\gamma}(\Delta_{i-1}^{\leq}(s_{i-1,j}))+1,n]\ &\text{if }\gamma|_{\Delta_{i}\cap\Gamma_{1}}>0;\\
[(\alpha_{i})_{+}(\Gamma_{2})+1,n]\ &\text{if }\gamma|_{\Delta_{i}\cap\Gamma_{1}}<0.\end{cases}

\displaystyle i\in[1,m],\ j\in[\alpha_{i}+1,n];

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{i}(j)

\displaystyle:={\Delta_{i}^{\leq}(s_{ij})\choose|\Delta_{i}\cap[\alpha_{i}+1,s_{ij}]|},\ \ \ \mathcal{N}_{i}:={\Delta_{i}\choose|\Delta_{i}^{\geq}(\alpha_{i}+1)|},

\displaystyle i\in[0,m-1],\ j\in[\alpha_{i}+1,n].

Proposition 6.4
.

For each
i\in[0,m]
and each
j\in[\alpha_{i}+1,n]
, the
h
-variable
h_{\alpha_{i}+1,j}
is given by:

1.

If
i=m
, then

h_{\alpha_{m}+1,j}(U)=(-1)^{\varepsilon_{\alpha_{m}+1,j}}\det U^{[j,n]}_{[\alpha_{m}+1,s_{mj}]};

(6.11)

2.

If
i=m-1
, then

h_{\alpha_{m-1}+1,j}(U)=(-1)^{\varepsilon_{\alpha_{m-1}+1,j}}\hskip-30.0pt\sum_{I_{m-1}:\,I_{m-1}\in\mathcal{N}_{m-1}(j)}\det U^{[j,n]}_{I_{m-1}\cup K_{m-1}^{\leq}(s_{m-1,j})}\det U^{\check{\gamma}(I_{m-1})\cup L_{m}^{\prime}(j)}_{[\alpha_{m}+1,n]};

(6.12)

3.

If
i\in[0,m-2]
, then

\displaystyle h_{\alpha_{i}+1,j}

\displaystyle(U)=

(6.13)

\displaystyle=(-1)^{\varepsilon_{\alpha_{i}+1,j}}\hskip-20.0pt\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(I_{i},I_{i+1},\ldots,I_{m-1}):\\
I_{i}\in\mathcal{N}_{i}(j),\ I_{t}\in\mathcal{N}_{t},\\
\ t\in[i+1,m-1]\end{subarray}}

\displaystyle\det U^{[j,n]}_{I_{i}\cup K_{i}^{\leq}(s_{ij})}\det U^{\check{\gamma}(I_{i})\cup L_{i+1}^{\prime}(j)}_{I_{i+1}\cup K_{i+1}}\det U^{\check{\gamma}(I_{i+1})\cup L_{i+2}}_{I_{i+2}\cup K_{i+2}}\cdots

\displaystyle\cdots\det U^{\check{\gamma}(I_{m-2})\cup L_{m-1}}_{I_{m-1}\cup K_{m-1}}\det U^{\check{\gamma}(I_{m-1})\cup L_{m}}_{[\alpha_{m}+1,n]}.

Proof.

The case
i=m
is already contained in Lemma
6.1
. Comparing (
6.1
) with
L_{i}^{\prime}(j)
from (
6.10
), we see that
L_{\alpha_{i}+1,s_{ij}}=L_{i+1}^{\prime}(j)
. Rewriting (
6.3
) in terms of (
6.10
), we see that

\begin{split}\det[\mathcal{F}_{m-i}(U)]_{[\alpha_{i}+1,s_{ij}]}^{[j,n]}\cdot&\det[\mathcal{F}_{m-i-1}(U)]_{[\alpha_{i+1}+1,n]}^{[\alpha_{i+1}+1,n]}=\\
=&\sum_{I_{i}\in\mathcal{N}_{i}(j)}\det U^{[j,n]}_{I_{i}\cup K_{i}^{\leq}(s_{ij})}\det[\mathcal{F}_{m-i-1}(U)]_{[\alpha_{i+1}+1,n]}^{\check{\gamma}(I_{i})\cup L^{\prime}_{i+1}(j)}.\end{split}

(6.14)

Setting
i=m-1
in formula (
6.14
), we obtain (up to a sign) formula (
6.12
). If
m-i-1\geq 1
, let us expand
\det[\mathcal{F}_{m-i-1}(U)]_{[\alpha_{i+1}+1,n]}^{\check{\gamma}(I_{i})\cup L_{i+1}^{\prime}(j)}
via formula (
6.3
); in the notation of (
6.10
), we obtain

\begin{split}\det[\mathcal{F}_{m-i}(U)&]_{[\alpha_{i}+1,s_{ij}]}^{[j,n]}\det[\mathcal{F}_{m-i-1}(U)]_{[\alpha_{i+1}+1,n]}^{[\alpha_{i+1}+1,n]}\det[\mathcal{F}_{m-i-2}(U)]_{[\alpha_{i+2}+1,n]}^{[\alpha_{i+2}+1,n]}=\\
=&\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}I_{i}\in\mathcal{N}_{i}(j),\\
I_{i+1}\in\mathcal{N}_{i+1}\end{subarray}}\det U^{[j,n]}_{I_{i}\cup K_{i}^{\leq}(s_{ij})}\det U_{[\alpha_{i+1}+1,n]}^{\check{\gamma}(I_{i})\cup L^{\prime}_{i+1}(j)}\det[\mathcal{F}_{m-i-2}(U)]_{[\alpha_{i+2}+1,n]}^{\check{\gamma}(I_{i+1})\cup L_{i+2}}.\end{split}

(6.15)

Now, if
m-i-2\geq 1
, we apply formula (
6.3
) again to the term
\det[\mathcal{F}_{m-i-2}(U)]_{[\alpha_{i+2}+1,n]}^{\check{\gamma}(I_{i+1})\cup L_{i+2}}
, and so with
\det[\mathcal{F}_{m-i-3}(U)]_{[\alpha_{i+3}+1,n]}^{\check{\gamma}(I_{i+2})\cup L_{i+3}}
in the resulting formula if
m-i-3\geq 1
. Continuing in this fashion, we will finally reach the terms
\det[\mathcal{F}_{0}(U)]_{[\alpha_{m}+1,n]}^{\check{\gamma}(I_{m-1})\cup L_{m}}
, which are equal to
\det U_{[\alpha_{m}+1,n]}^{\check{\gamma}(I_{m-1})\cup L_{m}}
. By Proposition
6.3
, the right-hand side will be equal (up to a sign) to
h_{\alpha_{i}+1,j}(U)
, and the left-hand side will be the desired expansion in terms of minors of
U
.
∎

Corollary 6.5
.

The polynomial degree of
h_{\alpha_{i}+1,j}
is given by

\deg h_{\alpha_{i}+1,j}=n-j+1+\sum_{\ell=1}^{m-i}(n-\alpha_{i+\ell}).

(6.16)

6.3
Duality of the frozen
h
-variables

Let
\mathbf{\Gamma}:=(\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2},\gamma)
be a BD triple and
\mathbf{\Gamma}^{\mathrm{op}}:=(\Gamma_{2},\Gamma_{1},\gamma^{*})
be the opposite BD triple. In this subsection, we prove a certain duality property between the frozen
h
-variables in
\mathcal{GC}_{h}^{\dagger}(\mathbf{\Gamma})
and
\mathcal{GC}_{h}^{\dagger}(\mathbf{\Gamma}^{\mathrm{op}})
. We will subsequently use this property in two instances: 1) to prove Proposition
1.8
(semi-invariance of the frozen
h
-variables); 2) to show that the frozen variables in
\mathcal{GC}_{h}^{\dagger}(\mathbf{\Gamma})
coincide with the frozen variables in
\mathcal{GC}_{g}^{\dagger}(\mathbf{\Gamma})
(Proposition
7.6
).

Proposition 6.6
.

Let
S^{\gamma}(\alpha_{0}):=\{\alpha_{i}\}_{i=0}^{m}
and
S^{\gamma^{*}}(\beta_{0}):=\{\beta_{i}\}_{i=0}^{m}
be a
\gamma
- and a
\gamma^{*}
-string that start at
\alpha_{0}\in\Pi\setminus\Gamma_{2}
and
\beta_{0}\in\Pi\setminus\Gamma_{1}
, and such that
\beta_{0}=\alpha_{m}
. Let
h_{\alpha_{0}+1,\alpha_{0}+1}
be the frozen
h
-variable in
\mathcal{GC}_{h}^{\dagger}(\mathbf{\Gamma})
associated with
\alpha_{0}
and
\bar{h}_{\beta_{0}+1,\beta_{0}+1}
be the frozen
h
-variable in
\mathcal{GC}_{h}^{\dagger}(\mathbf{\Gamma}^{\mathrm{op}})
associated with
\beta_{0}
. Then

\bar{h}_{\beta_{0}+1,\beta_{0}+1}(U^{T})=h_{\alpha_{0}+1,\alpha_{0}+1}(U).

(6.17)

Proof.

We will compare the explicit expansions of the variables in terms of minors of
U
. Consider the data (
6.10
) relative the BD triple
\mathbf{\Gamma}^{\mathrm{op}}
for the
\gamma^{*}
-string
S^{\gamma^{*}}(\beta_{0})
; to distinguish it from the data for
S^{\gamma}(\alpha_{0})
, let us decorate the former with a bar; for instance, we set
\bar{K}_{i}:=[(\beta_{i})_{+}(\Gamma_{2})+1,n]
. Now, if
m=0
, the statement is trivial; for
m>0
, it follows from Proposition
6.4
that both variables can be expanded as

\bar{h}_{\beta_{0}+1,\beta_{0}+1}(U)=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(\bar{I}_{0},\bar{I}_{1},\ldots,\bar{I}_{m-1}):\\
\bar{I}_{i}\in\bar{\mathcal{N}}_{i},\,0\leq i\leq m-1\end{subarray}}\det U_{\bar{I}_{0}\cup\bar{K}_{0}}^{[\beta_{0},n]}\left[\prod_{i=1}^{m-1}\det U_{\bar{I}_{i}\cup\bar{K}_{i}}^{\check{\gamma}^{*}(\bar{I}_{i-1})\cup\bar{L}_{i}}\right]\det U^{\check{\gamma}^{*}(\bar{I}_{m-1})\cup\bar{L}_{m}}_{[\beta_{m},n]};

(6.18)

h_{\alpha_{0}+1,\alpha_{0}+1}(U)=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(I_{0},I_{1},\ldots,I_{m-1}):\\
I_{i}\in\mathcal{N}_{i},\,0\leq i\leq m-1\end{subarray}}\det U^{[\alpha_{0},n]}_{I_{0}\cup K_{0}}\left[\prod_{i=1}^{m-1}\det U^{\check{\gamma}(I_{i-1})\cup L_{i}}_{I_{i}\cup K_{i}}\right]\det U_{[\alpha_{m},n]}^{\check{\gamma}(I_{m-1})\cup L_{m}}.

(6.19)

Let us establish a connection between the data associated with the two variables. We see that for each
i\in[0,m]
,
\beta_{i}=\alpha_{m-i}
,
\bar{K}_{i}=L_{m-i}
,
\bar{L}_{i}=K_{m-i}
; as for
\mathcal{N}_{i}
, we see that for each
i\in[0,m-1]
,

\check{\gamma}|_{\mathcal{N}_{i}}:\mathcal{N}_{i}\rightarrow\bar{\mathcal{N}}_{m-i-1}

is a bijection; therefore, in formula (
6.18
) we can substitute each
\bar{I}_{i}
with
\check{\gamma}(I_{m-i-1})
and each
\check{\gamma}^{*}(\bar{I}_{i-1})
with
I_{m-i}
. Now, combining the latter with the substitution
U\mapsto U^{T}
, formula (
6.17
) follows.
∎

Corollary 6.7
.

Let
h_{ii}
be a frozen
h
-variable in
\mathcal{GC}_{h}^{\dagger}(\mathbf{\Gamma})
, and let
P
be a unipotent upper triangular matrix. Then the following identity holds:

h_{ii}(PU\tilde{\gamma}(P)^{-1})=h_{ii}(U).

(6.20)

Proof.

By Proposition
6.6
, there exists a frozen
h
-variable
\bar{h}_{jj}
in
\mathcal{GC}_{h}^{\dagger}(\mathbf{\Gamma}^{\mathrm{op}})
such that
h_{ii}(U)=\bar{h}_{jj}(U^{T})
. Since
P^{T}
is a unipotent lower triangular matrix, applying formula (
4.20
), we see that

h_{ii}(U)=\bar{h}_{jj}(U^{T})=\bar{h}_{jj}(\tilde{\gamma}(P^{-T})U^{T}P^{T})=h_{ii}(PU\tilde{\gamma}(P)^{-1}).

(6.21)

Thus formula (
6.20
) holds. ∎

Corollary 6.8
.

Let
h_{ii}
be a frozen
h
-variable in
\mathcal{GC}_{h}^{\dagger}(\mathbf{\Gamma})
. Then for any
U\in\GL_{n}
,

U\nabla_{U}h_{ii}-\mathring{\gamma}^{*}\left(\nabla_{U}h_{ii}\cdot U\right)\in\mathfrak{b}_{+}.

(6.22)

Proof.

The statement follows from differentiating formula (
6.20
).
∎

6.4
Semi-invariance of the frozen
h
-variables

In this subsection, we prove Proposition
1.8
for
\mathcal{GC}_{h}^{\dagger}(\mathbf{\Gamma})
, and we also show that the frozen
h
-variables do not vanish on
\SL_{n}^{\dagger}
. Let
\mathbf{\Gamma}:=(\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2},\gamma)
be a BD triple of type
A_{n-1}
. We set
\mathcal{P}_{+}(\Gamma_{2})
and
\mathcal{P}_{-}(\Gamma_{1})
to be the parabolic subgroups of
\GL_{n}
generated by the BD data
\mathbf{\Gamma}
. The subgroup
\mathcal{D}\subset\mathcal{P}_{+}(\Gamma_{1})\times\mathcal{P}_{-}(\Gamma_{2})
is defined as

\mathcal{D}:=\{(g^{\prime},g)\in\mathcal{P}_{+}(\Gamma_{1})\times\mathcal{P}_{-}(\Gamma_{2})\ |\ {\mathring{\phantom{\gamma}}\kern-6.3pt\tilde{\gamma}}(\mathring{\Pi}_{\Gamma_{1}}(g^{\prime}))=\mathring{\Pi}_{\Gamma_{2}}(g)\}.

(6.23)

The group
\mathcal{D}
acts upon
\GL_{n}
via

(g^{\prime},g).U=g^{\prime}Ug^{-1},\ \ (g^{\prime},g)\in\mathcal{D},\ U\in\GL_{n}.

(6.24)

Proposition
.

Let
\psi
be a frozen variable in
\mathcal{GC}^{\dagger}_{h}(\mathbf{\Gamma},G)
,
G\in\{\SL_{n},\GL_{n}\}
. Then the following statements hold:

1)

The variable
\psi
is semi-invariant with respect to the action (
6.24
);

2)

The variable
\psi
is log-canonical with any
u_{ij}
;

3)

The zero locus of
\psi
foliates into a union of symplectic leaves of
(G,\pi_{\mathbf{\Gamma}}^{\dagger})
.

Proof.

The statements are evident for the
c
-variables, which are Casimirs of the Poisson bracket. Part
3
for the
c
-variables follows from Proposition
2.5
and the fact that the
c
-variables, as elements of
\mathcal{O}(\GL_{n})
, are irreducible (see
[
20
, Theorem 3.10]
). From now on, we assume that
\psi
is a frozen
h
-variable.

1)

Pick a pair
(g^{\prime},g)\in\mathcal{D}
and decompose it as
g^{\prime}=g^{\prime}_{+}g^{\prime}_{0}g^{\prime}_{-}
and
g=g_{+}g_{0}g_{-}
, where the subscript indicates if the matrix is unipotent upper, diagonal or unipotent lower, respectively. The defining condition of
\mathcal{D}
implies that
{\mathring{\phantom{\gamma}}\kern-6.3pt\tilde{\gamma}}^{*}(g_{-})=g^{\prime}_{-}
and
{\mathring{\phantom{\gamma}}\kern-6.3pt\tilde{\gamma}}(g^{\prime}_{+})=g_{+}
. It follows from Corollary
4.10
and Corollary
6.7
that

\psi(g^{\prime}Ug^{-1})=\psi\left(g^{\prime}_{+}g^{\prime}_{0}{\mathring{\phantom{\gamma}}\kern-6.3pt\tilde{\gamma}}^{*}(g_{-})U\,\hskip-3.0pt\left.g_{-}\hskip-4.0pt\right.^{-1}\,\hskip-3.0pt\left.g_{0}\hskip-4.0pt\right.^{-1}{\mathring{\phantom{\gamma}}\kern-6.3pt\tilde{\gamma}}(g^{\prime}_{+})^{-1}\right)=\chi_{\psi}(g^{\prime},g)\psi(U)

(6.25)

where
\chi_{\psi}
is a character on
\mathcal{D}
. Thus
\psi
is semi-invariant with respect to the action of
\mathcal{D}
.

2)

For convenience, let us choose
\pi_{\mathbf{\Gamma}}^{\dagger}
with a ringed
19
19

19

As we showed in Section 8.2 in
[
31
]
, log-canonicity does not depend on the choice of
R_{0}
.

R_{0}
(see formula (
4.8
)). Let us set

\displaystyle\mathring{\nu}(\psi)

\displaystyle:=\nabla_{U}\psi\cdot U-\mathring{\gamma}(U\nabla_{U}\psi);

(6.26)

\displaystyle\mathring{\nu}^{*}(\psi)

\displaystyle:=U\nabla_{U}\psi-\mathring{\gamma}^{*}(\nabla_{U}\psi\cdot U).

(6.27)

It follows from Corollary
4.11
and Corollary
6.8
that
\mathring{\nu}(\psi)\in\mathfrak{b}_{-}
,
\mathring{\nu}^{*}(\psi)\in\mathfrak{b}_{+}
,
\pi_{0}(\mathring{\nu}(\log\psi))=\mathrm{const}
and
\pi_{0}(\mathring{\nu}^{*}(\log\psi))=\mathrm{const}
. Moreover, one can write

\displaystyle\mathring{\nu}(\psi)

\displaystyle=[\nabla_{U}\psi,U]+(1-\mathring{\gamma})(U\nabla_{U}\psi);

(6.28)

\displaystyle\mathring{\nu}^{*}(\psi)

\displaystyle=-[\nabla_{U}\psi,U]+(1-\mathring{\gamma}^{*})(\nabla_{U}\psi\cdot U).

It follows that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{1-\mathring{\gamma}}\pi_{>}[\nabla_{U}\psi,U]

\displaystyle=-\pi_{>}(U\nabla_{U}\psi);

(6.29)

\displaystyle\frac{\mathring{\gamma}^{*}}{1-\mathring{\gamma}^{*}}\pi_{<}[\nabla_{U}h,U]

\displaystyle=\pi_{<}(U\nabla_{U}\psi).

(6.30)

Therefore,

R([\nabla_{U}\psi,U])=-\pi_{>}(U\nabla_{U}\psi)-\pi_{<}(U\nabla_{U}\psi)+\mathring{R}_{0}\pi_{0}[\nabla_{U}\psi,U].

(6.31)

Rewriting the last piece, we see that

\begin{split}\mathring{R}_{0}\pi_{0}[\nabla_{U}\psi,U]=&\mathring{R}_{0}\pi_{0}\mathring{\nu}(\psi)-\mathring{R}_{0}(1-\mathring{\gamma})(U\nabla_{U}\psi)=\\
=&\mathring{R}_{0}\pi_{0}\mathring{\nu}(\psi)-\pi_{0}\mathring{\pi}_{\Gamma_{1}}(U\nabla_{U}\psi)-\mathring{R}_{0}\pi_{0}\mathring{\pi}_{\hat{\Gamma}_{1}}(U\nabla_{U}\psi)=\\
=&\mathring{R}_{0}\pi_{0}\mathring{\nu}(\psi)-\pi_{0}(U\nabla_{U}\psi)+\mathring{R}_{0}^{*}\pi_{0}\mathring{\pi}_{\hat{\Gamma}_{1}}(U\nabla_{U}\psi);\end{split}

(6.32)

hence, formula (
6.31
) can be updated as

R([\nabla_{U}\psi,U])=-U\nabla_{U}\psi+\mathring{R}_{0}\pi_{0}\mathring{\nu}(\psi)+\mathring{R}_{0}^{*}\pi_{0}\mathring{\pi}_{\hat{\Gamma}_{1}}(U\nabla_{U}\psi).

(6.33)

Now, the Poisson bracket between
\psi
and
u_{ij}
is given by

\begin{split}\{\psi,u_{ij}\}_{\mathbf{\Gamma}}^{\dagger}=&\langle R([\nabla_{U}\psi,U]),[\nabla_{U}u_{ij},U]\rangle-\langle[\nabla_{U}\psi,U],\nabla_{U}u_{ij}U\rangle=\\
=&-\langle U\nabla_{U}\psi,[\nabla_{U}u_{ij},U]\rangle-\langle[\nabla_{U}\psi,U],\nabla_{U}u_{ij}U\rangle+\\
+&\langle\mathring{R}_{0}\pi_{0}\mathring{\nu}(\psi)+\mathring{R}_{0}^{*}\pi_{0}\mathring{\pi}_{\hat{\Gamma}_{1}}(U\nabla_{U}\psi),\pi_{0}[\nabla_{U}u_{ij},U]\rangle.\end{split}

(6.34)

The first two terms in the last identity cancel each other out. As for the last term, notice that
\pi_{0}[\nabla_{U}u_{ij},U]=u_{ij}(e_{jj}-e_{ii})
, and since
\mathring{\nu}(\log\psi)=\mathrm{const}
and
\mathring{\nu}^{*}(\log\psi)=\mathrm{const}
, we finally conclude that
\{\psi,u_{ij}\}_{\mathbf{\Gamma}}^{\dagger}
is proportional to the product
\psi\cdot u_{ij}
.

3)

It is a consequence of Proposition
5.9
that
\psi
is a prime element of
\mathcal{O}(G)
, and it follows from Part
2
of the current proposition that the principal ideal
(\psi)
is Poisson. Now the statement follows from Proposition
2.5
.∎

Remark 6.9
.

Let
\mathbf{\Gamma}
be an aperiodic oriented BD triple and let
\mathcal{GC}^{D}(\mathbf{\Gamma},\mathbf{\Gamma})
be the generalized cluster structure on
D(\GL_{n})
constructed in
[
31
]
. Then any frozen
g
- or
h
-variable in
\mathcal{GC}^{D}(\mathbf{\Gamma},\mathbf{\Gamma})
is semi-invariant with respect to both the right and the left action of
\mathcal{D}
upon
D(\GL_{n})
.
Therefore, the frozen variables in
\mathcal{GC}^{D}(\mathbf{\Gamma},\mathbf{\Gamma})
and in
\mathcal{GC}^{\dagger}_{h}(\mathbf{\Gamma},\GL_{n})
are semi-invariant with respect to the same group.

Proposition 6.10
.

The following statements hold:

1)

For any choice of
r_{0}
, the frozen
h
-variables in
\mathcal{GC}^{\dagger}_{h}(\mathbf{\Gamma},\SL_{n})
do not vanish on
\SL_{n}^{\dagger}
;

2)

If
r_{0}
is ringed, then the frozen
h
-variables in
\mathcal{GC}^{\dagger}(\mathbf{\Gamma},\GL_{n})
do not vanish on
\GL_{n}^{\dagger}
.

Proof.

Both statements can be proved in the same way, so let us focus on the case of
\GL_{n}
. Let
r_{0}
be chosen as a ringed element (see formula (
4.8
)), and let
\psi
be a frozen
h
-variable in
\mathcal{GC}_{h}^{\dagger}(\mathbf{\Gamma},\GL_{n})
. We set
\tilde{\psi}\in\mathcal{O}(D(\GL_{n}))
to be a lift of
\psi
given by

\tilde{\psi}(X,Y):=\psi(X^{-1}Y),\ \ (X,Y)\in D(\GL_{n}).

(6.35)

The choice of
r_{0}
implies that
\mathring{\gamma}R_{+}=\mathring{\pi}_{\Gamma_{2}}R_{-}
, hence
\GL_{n}^{*}\subseteq\mathcal{D}
, where
\GL_{n}^{*}
is viewed as an immersed Lie subgroup of
D(\GL_{n})
. We see that

\tilde{\psi}(g^{\prime},g)=\psi((g^{\prime})^{-1}g)=\chi_{\psi}(g^{\prime},g)\psi(I)

(6.36)

where
\chi_{\psi}
is a character on
\mathcal{D}
from Proposition
1.8
. Since
\mathcal{F}(I)=I
, it follows from the defining formula for the
h
-functions (
1.11
) that
\psi(I)\neq 0
. Hence
\tilde{\psi}|_{\mathcal{D}}
does not vanish at any point of
\mathcal{D}
, and thus
\psi
is nonzero on
\GL_{n}^{\dagger}
and
\SL_{n}^{\dagger}
.
∎

Remark 6.11
.

Proposition
6.10
justifies the choice of the ground ring for
\mathcal{GC}_{h}^{\dagger}(\mathbf{\Gamma},\SL_{n}^{\dagger})
(see formula (
1.19
)). For
\mathcal{GC}_{h}^{\dagger}(\mathbf{\Gamma},\GL_{n}^{\dagger})
, if
r_{0}
is ringed, then one chooses

\hat{\mathbb{A}}_{\mathbb{C}}(\mathbf{\Gamma},\GL_{n}^{\dagger}):=\mathbb{C}[h_{11}^{\pm 1},c_{1},\ldots,c_{n-1},h_{i+1,i+1}^{\pm 1}\ |\ i\in\Pi\setminus\Gamma_{2}].

(6.37)

7
The
g
-convention

In this section, for any Belavin-Drinfeld triple
\mathbf{\Gamma}
of type
A_{n-1}
, we describe the generalized cluster structure
\mathcal{GC}^{\dagger}_{g}(\mathbf{\Gamma})
on
\GL_{n}
(and on the related varieties
\SL_{n}^{\dagger}
,
\SL_{n}
,
\GL_{n}^{\dagger}
) in the
g
-convention
.

7.1
The map
\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{op}}

In this subsection, we recast the map
\mathcal{F}
from Section
4.2
in the
g
-convention. For a fixed set of simple roots
\Pi
of type
A_{n-1}
, let
\mathbf{\Gamma}:=(\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2},\gamma)
be a BD triple,
\mathcal{B}_{\pm}
,
\mathcal{N}_{\pm}
be the Borel subgroups and their unipotent radicals,
\mathcal{H}
be the corresponding Cartan subgroup of
\GL_{n}
. We decompose a generic element
U\in\GL_{n}
as
U=U_{\oplus}U_{-}=U_{+}U_{\ominus}
, where
U_{\ominus}\in\mathcal{B}_{-}
,
U_{\oplus}\in\mathcal{B}_{+}
,
U_{-}\in\mathcal{N}_{-}
,
U_{+}\in\mathcal{N}_{+}
. Define a sequence of rational maps
\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{op}}_{k}:\GL_{n}\dashrightarrow\GL_{n}
via
20
20

20

To avoid confusion, let us emphasize that formula (
7.1
) defines
\mathcal{F}_{k}^{\mathrm{op}}
relative the BD triple
\mathbf{\Gamma}
and not
\mathbf{\Gamma}^{\mathrm{op}}
. If
\mathcal{F}_{k}^{\mathrm{op}}
is defined relative the opposite BD triple
\mathbf{\Gamma}^{\mathrm{op}}
, then it satisfies the recurrence relation
\mathcal{F}_{k}^{\mathrm{op}}(U)=U\tilde{\gamma}^{*}(\mathcal{F}_{k-1}(U)_{+})
.

\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{op}}_{0}(U):=U,\ \ \mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{op}}_{k}(U)=U\tilde{\gamma}(\mathcal{F}_{k-1}(U)_{+}),\ \ U\in\GL_{n},\ k\geq 1.

(7.1)

The rational map
\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{op}}:\GL_{n}\dashrightarrow\GL_{n}
is defined as the limit

\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{op}}(U):=\lim_{k\rightarrow\infty}\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{op}}_{k}(U),\ \ U\in\GL_{n}.

(7.2)

The sequence
\mathcal{F}_{k}^{\mathrm{op}}
stabilizes at
k=\deg\gamma
. As a corollary,
\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{op}}(U)
satisfies the relation

\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{op}}(U)=U\tilde{\gamma}(\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{op}}(U)_{+}),

(7.3)

and
\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{op}}
is a birational map with the inverse given by

(\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{op}})^{-1}(U)=U\tilde{\gamma}(U_{+})^{-1}.

(7.4)

Let
T:\GL_{n}\rightarrow\GL_{n}
be the transposition map; that is,
T(U)=U^{T}
,
U\in\GL_{n}
.

Proposition 7.1
.

Let
\mathcal{F}:\GL_{n}\dashrightarrow\GL_{n}
and
\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{op}}:\GL_{n}\dashrightarrow\GL_{n}
be constructed relative the BD triples
\mathbf{\Gamma}
and
\mathbf{\Gamma}^{\mathrm{op}}
, respectively. Then for any
k\geq 0
the following diagram commutes:

\textstyle{\GL_{n}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{T}
\scriptstyle{\mathcal{F}_{k}}
\textstyle{\GL_{n}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{T}
\textstyle{\GL_{n}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathcal{F}_{k}^{\mathrm{op}}}
\textstyle{\GL_{n}}

(7.5)

Proof.

Indeed, the commutativity is evident for
k=0
. Assuming by induction that the commutativity holds for
k
, we see that

[\mathcal{F}_{k+1}(U^{T})]^{T}=\left[\tilde{\gamma}^{*}(\mathcal{F}_{k}(U^{T})_{-})U^{T}\right]^{T}=U\tilde{\gamma}^{*}\left[(\mathcal{F}_{k}(U^{T})^{T})_{+}\right]=U\tilde{\gamma}^{*}(\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{op}}_{k}(U)_{+})=\mathcal{F}_{k+1}^{\mathrm{op}}(U).

(7.6)

Thus the diagram commutes.
∎

Corollary 7.2
.

For
P\in\mathcal{N}_{+}
and
T\in\mathcal{H}
, the map
\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{op}}
enjoys the following invariance properties:

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{op}}(PU\tilde{\gamma}(P)^{-1})=P\cdot\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{op}}(U);

(7.7)

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{op}}({\mathring{\phantom{\gamma}}\kern-6.3pt\tilde{\gamma}}^{*}(T)UT^{-1})={\mathring{\phantom{\gamma}}\kern-6.3pt\tilde{\gamma}}^{*}(T)\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{op}}(U)T^{-1};

(7.8)

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{op}}(T^{-1}U{\mathring{\phantom{\gamma}}\kern-6.3pt\tilde{\gamma}}(T))=T^{-1}\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{op}}(U){\mathring{\phantom{\gamma}}\kern-6.3pt\tilde{\gamma}}(T).

(7.9)

Proof.

The identities follow from Corollary
4.9
and Proposition
7.1
.
∎

7.2
Description of
\mathcal{GC}_{g}^{\dagger}(\mathbf{\Gamma})

In this subsection, we describe the initial extended cluster of
\mathcal{GC}_{g}^{\dagger}(\mathbf{\Gamma})
for an arbitrary BD triple
\mathbf{\Gamma}:=(\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2},\gamma)
of type
A_{n-1}
. It comprises three types of functions:
c
-functions,
\phi
-functions and
g
-functions. The initial quiver is described in Appendix
B
.

Description of
\phi
- and
c
-functions.

The
c
-functions are given by (
1.7
) (as in the
h
-convention). The
\phi
-functions are similar to the
\varphi
-functions and are defined as follows. For an element
U\in\GL_{n}
, let us set

\Phi_{kl}^{\prime}(U):=\begin{bmatrix}(U^{0})^{[1,k]}&U^{[1,l]}&(U^{2})^{\{1\}}&\cdots&(U^{n-k-l+1})^{\{1\}}\end{bmatrix},\ \ k,l\geq 1,\ k+l\leq n;

(7.10)

Then the
\phi
-functions are given by

\phi_{kl}(U):=s_{kl}\det\Phi_{kl}^{\prime}(U)

(7.11)

where
s_{kl}\in\{1,-1\}
is a sign given by (
1.4
) (as in the
h
-convention). For
P\in\mathcal{N}_{+}
and
T\in\mathcal{H}
, the
\phi
-functions enjoy the following invariance properties:

\displaystyle\phi_{kl}(PUP^{-1})

\displaystyle=\phi_{kl}(U),

(7.12)

\displaystyle\phi_{kl}(TUT^{-1})

\displaystyle=\chi_{\phi_{kl}}(T)\phi_{kl}(U)

where
\chi_{\phi_{kl}}
is a character.

Remark 7.3
.

The
\varphi
- and
\phi
-functions are related as follows. Set
W_{0}:=\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}(-1)^{i+1}e_{n-i+1,i}
and consider the map
w_{0}:\GL_{n}\rightarrow\GL_{n}
,
w_{0}(U)=W_{0}^{-1}UW_{0}
. If
\phi_{kl}
is a
\phi
-function, then
\phi_{kl}(w_{0}(U))=\varphi_{kl}(U)
is a
\varphi
-function. It follows that the
\phi
-functions are irreducible as elements of
\mathcal{O}(\GL_{n})
, and the mutations of
\phi_{kl}
for
2\leq k+l\leq n-1
are regular and coprime with
\phi_{kl}
. Moreover, if
\mathcal{O}(\GL_{n})^{\mathcal{N}_{\pm}}
are the rings of regular functions invariant with respect to the adjoint actions of
\mathcal{N}_{+}
and
\mathcal{N}_{-}
, then
\omega_{0}^{*}:(\mathcal{O}(\GL_{n})^{\mathcal{N}_{+}},\{\cdot,\cdot\}_{\mathbf{\Gamma}}^{\dagger})\xrightarrow{\sim}(\mathcal{O}(\GL_{n})^{\mathcal{N}_{-}},\{\cdot,\cdot\}_{\mathbf{\Gamma}}^{\dagger})
is a Poisson isomorphism for any
\mathbf{\Gamma}
. In particular, the
\phi
-functions are log-canonical.

Remark 7.4
.

The
\phi
-functions have appeared before in
[
22
]
. They were used to define a generalized cluster structure on the Drinfeld double
D(\GL_{n})=\GL_{n}\times\GL_{n}
, which is an alternative to the use of the
\varphi
-functions in
[
20
]
. It was shown in
[
21
]
that the resulting constructions with
\phi
- and
\varphi
-functions (for the trivial BD triples) provide equivalent generalized cluster structures in
n=2
and
n=3
; however, they are not equivalent in
n=4
(in other words, the initial extended seeds are not mutation equivalent). In the supplementary note
[
33
]
, we show that
\mathcal{GC}_{g}^{\dagger}(\mathbf{\Gamma})
and
\mathcal{GC}_{h}^{\dagger}(\mathbf{\Gamma}^{\mathrm{op}})
are equivalent for
n=3
and any BD triple
\mathbf{\Gamma}
. We conjecture that they are not equivalent for
n\geq 4
.

Description of the
g
-functions.

Let
\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{op}}:\GL_{n}\dashrightarrow\GL_{n}
be the birational map defined in (
7.2
). For each
\alpha_{0}\in\Pi\setminus\Gamma_{1}
and the associated
\gamma^{*}
-string
S^{\gamma^{*}}(\alpha_{0}):=\{\alpha_{i}\}_{i=0}^{m}
(see Definition
1.2
), for every
k\in[0,m]
and
i\in[\alpha_{k}+1,n]
, set

g_{i,\alpha_{k}+1}(U):=\det[\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{op}}(U)]_{[i,n]}^{[\alpha_{k}+1,n-i+\alpha_{k}+1]}\prod_{t\geq k+1}^{m}\det[\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{op}}(U)]_{[\alpha_{t}+1,n]}^{[\alpha_{t}+1,n]}.

(7.13)

It is evident from the above formula that the flag minors of
\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{op}}(U)
can be expressed as

\det[\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{op}}(U)]_{[i,n]}^{[j,n-i+j]}=\begin{cases}\frac{g_{ij}(U)}{g_{\mu\mu}(U)}\ &\text{if}\ j-1\in\Gamma_{2}\\
g_{ij}(U)\ &\text{otherwise}\end{cases}

(7.14)

where
1\leq i\leq n
,
1\leq j\leq i
and
\mu:=\gamma^{*}(j-1)+1
if
j-1\in\Gamma_{2}
.

Proposition 7.5
.

Let
g_{ij}
be a
g
-function from the initial extended cluster of
\mathcal{GC}_{g}^{\dagger}(\mathbf{\Gamma})
and
h_{ji}
be an
h
-function from the initial extended cluster of
\mathcal{GC}_{h}^{\dagger}(\mathbf{\Gamma}^{\mathrm{op}})
. Then
g_{ij}(U)=(-1)^{\varepsilon_{ji}}h_{ji}(U^{T})
, where
\varepsilon_{ji}=(i-j)(n-j)
.

Proof.

The statement is a consequence of Proposition
7.1
and the defining formulas (
1.11
) and (
7.13
) for the
h
- and
g
-functions.
∎

As a corollary, one can obtain an explicit expansion of the
g
-functions in terms of minors of
U
, as in Section
6.2
. One can also obtain invariance properties of the
g
-functions from Corollary
7.2
.

Frozen variables.

The frozen variables in
\mathcal{GC}_{g}^{\dagger}(\mathbf{\Gamma},\GL_{n})
are given by the set

\{c_{1},c_{2},\ldots,c_{n-1}\}\cup\{g_{i+1,i+1}\ |\ i\in\Pi\setminus\Gamma_{1}\}\cup\{g_{11}\}.

(7.15)

In the case of
\mathcal{GC}_{g}^{\dagger}(\mathbf{\Gamma},\SL_{n})
,
g_{11}(U)=1
, so this variable is absent. As the next proposition shows, the frozen variables in
\mathcal{GC}_{g}^{\dagger}(\mathbf{\Gamma})
and
\mathcal{GC}_{h}^{\dagger}(\mathbf{\Gamma})
are identical.

Proposition 7.6
.

Let
\{\alpha_{t}\}_{t=0}^{m}
be a
\gamma
-string for the BD triple
(\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2},\gamma)
. Then

h_{\alpha_{0}+1,\alpha_{0}+1}(U)=g_{\alpha_{m}+1,\alpha_{m}+1}(U)

(7.16)

where
h_{\alpha_{0}+1,\alpha_{0}+1}
is a frozen variable in
\mathcal{GC}_{h}^{\dagger}(\mathbf{\Gamma})
and
g_{\alpha_{m}+1,\alpha_{m}+1}
is a frozen variable in
\mathcal{GC}_{g}^{\dagger}(\mathbf{\Gamma})
.

Proof.

Let us denote by
\bar{h}
the
h
-variables in
\mathcal{GC}_{h}^{\dagger}(\mathbf{\Gamma}^{\mathrm{op}})
. Since
\alpha_{m}\notin\Gamma_{1}
, the variable
\bar{h}_{\alpha_{m}+1,\alpha_{m}+1}
is frozen in
\mathcal{GC}_{h}^{\dagger}(\mathbf{\Gamma}^{\mathrm{op}})
. It follows from Proposition
7.5
that
g_{\alpha_{m}+1,\alpha_{m}+1}(U)=\bar{h}_{\alpha_{m}+1,\alpha_{m}+1}(U^{T})
. By Proposition
6.6
,
\bar{h}_{\alpha_{m}+1,\alpha_{m}+1}(U^{T})=h_{\alpha_{0}+1,\alpha_{0}+1}(U)
. Thus the statement holds.
∎

The following proposition is a restatement of Proposition
1.8
in the case of
\mathcal{GC}_{g}^{\dagger}(\mathbf{\Gamma})
.

Proposition
.

Let
\psi
be a frozen variable in
\mathcal{GC}^{\dagger}_{g}(\mathbf{\Gamma},G)
,
G\in\{\SL_{n},\GL_{n}\}
. Then the following statements hold:

1)

The variable
\psi
is semi-invariant with respect to the action (
1.29
);

2)

The variable
\psi
is log-canonical with any
u_{ij}
;

3)

The zero locus of
\psi
foliates into a union of symplectic leaves of
(G,\pi_{\mathbf{\Gamma}}^{\dagger})
.

Proof.

It is a consequence of Proposition
1.8
for
\mathcal{GC}_{h}^{\dagger}(\mathbf{\Gamma})
(see Section
6.4
) and Proposition
7.6
.
∎

Initial extended cluster.

The initial extended cluster
\Psi_{0}
of
\mathcal{GC}^{\dagger}_{g}(\mathbf{\Gamma},\GL_{n})
is given by the set

\{g_{ij}\ |\ 2\leq j\leq i\leq n\}\cup\{\phi_{kl}\ |\ k,l\geq 1,k+l\leq n\}\cup\{c_{1},\ldots,c_{n-1}\}\cup\{g_{11}\}

(7.17)

where
g_{11}(U):=\det(U)
. In the case of
\SL_{n}
and
\SL_{n}^{\dagger}
, the initial extended cluster is the same except the variable
g_{11}
is removed.

Ground rings.

The ground rings are defined as follows.

\displaystyle\hat{\mathbb{A}}_{\mathbb{C}}(\mathbf{\Gamma},\GL_{n}):=

\displaystyle\mathbb{C}[g_{11}^{\pm 1},c_{1},\ldots,c_{n-1},g_{i+1,i+1}\ |\ i\in\Pi\setminus\Gamma_{1}];

(7.18)

\displaystyle\hat{\mathbb{A}}_{\mathbb{C}}(\mathbf{\Gamma},\SL_{n}):=

\displaystyle\mathbb{C}[c_{1},\ldots,c_{n-1},g_{i+1,i+1}\ |\ i\in\Pi\setminus\Gamma_{1}];

(7.19)

\displaystyle\hat{\mathbb{A}}_{\mathbb{C}}(\mathbf{\Gamma},\SL_{n}^{\dagger}):=

\displaystyle\mathbb{C}[c_{1},\ldots,c_{n-1},g_{i+1,i+1}^{\pm 1}\ |\ i\in\Pi\setminus\Gamma_{1}].

(7.20)

In the case of
\GL_{n}^{\dagger}
, if
r_{0}
is ringed, then one chooses

\hat{\mathbb{A}}_{\mathbb{C}}(\mathbf{\Gamma},\GL_{n}^{\dagger}):=\mathbb{C}[g_{11}^{\pm 1},c_{1},\ldots,c_{n-1},g_{i+1,i+1}^{\pm 1}\ |\ i\in\Pi\setminus\Gamma_{1}].

(7.21)

The choice of the ground rings is justified by Proposition
6.6
and Proposition
6.10
.

A generalized cluster mutation.

Only the variable
\phi_{11}
is equipped with a nontrivial string, which is given by
(1,c_{1},\ldots,c_{n-1},1)
. The generalized mutation relation for
\phi_{11}
reads

\phi_{11}\phi_{11}^{\prime}=\sum_{r=0}^{n}c_{r}\phi_{21}^{r}\phi_{12}^{n-r},

(7.22)

which is similar to the relation (
1.20
) in the
h
-convention. The mutations of all the other variables follow the usual pattern (
2.23
) from the theory of cluster algebras of geometric type.

7.3
Birational quasi-isomorphisms

In this subsection, we recast the birational quasi-isomorphisms from Section
4
in the
g
-convention. Let us recall from Section
3.5
that the rational map
\mathcal{Q}^{\mathrm{op}}:(\GL_{n},\pi_{\text{std}}^{\dagger})\dashrightarrow(\GL_{n},\pi_{\mathbf{\Gamma}}^{\dagger})
is given by

\mathcal{Q}^{\mathrm{op}}(U):=\rho^{\mathrm{op}}(U)U\rho^{\mathrm{op}}(U)^{-1},\ \ \rho^{\mathrm{op}}(U):=\prod_{i=1}^{\leftarrow}(\tilde{\gamma})^{i}(U_{+}),\ U\in\GL_{n}.

(7.23)

We showed in Proposition
3.15
that
\mathcal{Q}^{\mathrm{op}}
is a Poisson isomorphism provided the
r_{0}
parts of
\pi_{\text{std}}^{\dagger}
and
\pi_{\mathbf{\Gamma}}^{\dagger}
coincide. Define the rational map
\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{op},c}:(\GL_{n},\pi_{\mathbf{\Gamma}}^{\dagger})\dashrightarrow(\GL_{n},\pi_{\text{std}}^{\dagger})
as

\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{op},c}(U):=[\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{op}}(U)]^{-1}U\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{op}}(U)=\tilde{\gamma}(\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{op}}(U)_{+})^{-1}\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{op}}(U).

(7.24)

Proposition 7.7
.

The rational maps
\mathcal{Q}^{\mathrm{op}}
and
\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{op},c}
are inverse to each other.

Proof.

The statement follows from the commutative diagram (
3.55
) and Proposition
4.13
.
∎

Similarly to the
h
-convention, if
\tilde{\mathbf{\Gamma}}\prec\mathbf{\Gamma}
, then the
g
-variables in
\mathcal{GC}_{g}^{\dagger}(\tilde{\mathbf{\Gamma}})
are decorated with a tilde, and the marker for the pair
(\mathcal{GC}_{g}^{\dagger}(\mathbf{\Gamma}),\mathcal{GC}_{g}^{\dagger}(\tilde{\mathbf{\Gamma}}))
is chosen in such a way that each
\phi
- and
c
-function is related to itself, and each
g_{ij}
is related to
\tilde{g}_{ij}
.

Proposition 7.8
.

Let
\psi
be any variable from the initial extended cluster of
\mathcal{GC}^{\dagger}_{g}(\mathbf{\Gamma})
. Then the following identities hold:

1)

If
\psi
is a
\phi
- or
c
-function, then
(\mathcal{Q}^{\mathrm{op}})^{*}(\psi(U))=\psi(U)
;

2)

If
\psi=g_{ij}
for some
g
-function
g_{ij}(U)
, let
\{\alpha_{t}\}_{t=0}^{m}
be the
\gamma^{*}
-string such that
j-1=\alpha_{k}
for some
k
; then,

(\mathcal{Q}^{\mathrm{op}})^{*}(g_{ij}(U))=\tilde{g}_{ij}(U)\prod_{t\geq k+1}\tilde{g}_{\alpha_{t}+1,\alpha_{t}+1}(U).

(7.25)

Moreover,
\mathcal{Q}^{\mathrm{op}}:(G,\mathcal{GC}_{g}^{\dagger}(\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\text{std}}))\dashrightarrow(G,\mathcal{GC}_{g}^{\dagger}(\mathbf{\Gamma}))
is a birational quasi-isomorphism for
G\in\{\SL_{n},\GL_{n}\}
.

Proof.

Part
1
follows from the invariance properties of
\phi
- and
c
-functions. Part
2
follows from the commutative diagram (
3.55
), Proposition
4.15
and Proposition
7.5
. To show that
\mathcal{Q}^{\mathrm{op}}
is a birational quasi-isomorphism, one applies Proposition
2.25
and proceeds along the same lines as in the proof of Proposition
4.15
.
∎

Let
\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}^{\mathrm{op}}
denote the rational map
\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}^{\mathrm{op}}:(\GL_{n},\pi_{\text{std}}^{\dagger})\dashrightarrow(\GL_{n},\pi_{\tilde{\mathbf{\Gamma}}}^{\dagger})
given by formula (
7.23
) relative
\tilde{\mathbf{\Gamma}}
. The following proposition is a restatement of Proposition
4.17
for
\mathcal{GC}_{g}^{\dagger}(\mathbf{\Gamma})
, which in turn is a refinement of Proposition
1.7
.

Proposition 7.9
.

Given any pair
\mathbf{\Gamma}
and
\tilde{\mathbf{\Gamma}}
of BD triples of type
A_{n-1}
, set
\mathcal{G}^{\mathrm{op}}:=\mathcal{Q}^{\mathrm{op}}\circ(\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}^{\mathrm{op}})^{-1}
. Then
\mathcal{G}^{\mathrm{op}}
is a birational map given by the formula:

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}^{\mathrm{op}}(U)=G^{\mathrm{op}}(U)\cdot U\cdot G^{\mathrm{op}}(U)^{-1},\ \

\displaystyle G^{\mathrm{op}}(U):=\left[\prod_{i\geq 1}^{\leftarrow}(\tilde{\gamma})^{i}(G_{0}^{\mathrm{op}}(U))\right]\cdot G_{0}^{\mathrm{op}}(U),

(7.26)

\displaystyle G_{0}^{\mathrm{op}}(U):=\tilde{\gamma}(\tilde{\mathcal{F}}^{\mathrm{op}}(U)_{+})\tilde{\theta}(\tilde{\mathcal{F}}^{\mathrm{op}}(U)_{+})^{-1}.

(7.27)

Moreover, for
\tilde{\mathbf{\Gamma}}\prec\mathbf{\Gamma}
and
G\in\{\SL_{n},\GL_{n}\}
,
\mathcal{G}^{\mathrm{op}}:(G,\mathcal{GC}_{g}^{\dagger}(\tilde{\mathbf{\Gamma}}))\dashrightarrow(G,\mathcal{GC}_{g}^{\dagger}(\mathbf{\Gamma}))
is a birational quasi-isomorphism, and if the
r_{0}
parts of
\pi_{\tilde{\mathbf{\Gamma}}}^{\dagger}
and
\pi_{\mathbf{\Gamma}}^{\dagger}
coincide, then
\mathcal{G}^{\mathrm{op}}:(G,\pi_{\tilde{\mathbf{\Gamma}}}^{\dagger})\dashrightarrow(G,\pi_{\mathbf{\Gamma}}^{\dagger})
is a Poisson isomorphism.

Proof.

Formula (
7.26
) follows from Proposition
4.17
and the commutative diagram (
3.55
). The proof of the fact that
\mathcal{G}^{\mathrm{op}}
is a birational quasi-isomorphism follows along the same lines as the proof of Proposition
4.17
.
∎

Formula (
7.26
) also determines the inverse of
\mathcal{G}^{\mathrm{op}}
, as in Remark
4.18
. In the case
\tilde{\mathbf{\Gamma}}\prec\mathbf{\Gamma}
and
|\Gamma_{1}\setminus\tilde{\Gamma}_{1}|=1
, one can also derive a more explicit expression for
G_{0}^{\mathrm{op}}(U)
via Proposition
4.19
.

7.4
Proofs of Theorems
1.5
and
1.6
for
\mathcal{GC}_{g}^{\dagger}(\mathbf{\Gamma})

The proofs of Parts
2
,
3
and
4
of the theorems are subdivided into the three propositions below (Part
1
is then a consequence of Part
4
). We will transfer the results from the case of
\mathcal{GC}_{h}^{\dagger}(\mathbf{\Gamma}^{\mathrm{op}})
to
\mathcal{GC}_{g}^{\dagger}(\mathbf{\Gamma})
. The key is the following correspondence between the variables:

1)

For
\phi
- and
\varphi
-functions,
\phi_{kl}(W_{0}^{-1}UW_{0})=\varphi_{kl}(U)
(see Remark
7.3
);

2)

For
g_{ij}
and
h_{ji}
from the initial extended clusters of
\mathcal{GC}_{g}^{\dagger}(\mathbf{\Gamma})
and
\mathcal{GC}_{h}^{\dagger}(\mathbf{\Gamma}^{\mathrm{op}})
,
g_{ij}(U)=(-1)^{\varepsilon_{ji}}h_{ji}(U^{T})
(see Proposition
7.5
).

Both correspondences preserve the Poisson brackets (see Remark
7.3
and Lemma
3.14
).

Proposition 7.10
.

Let
\mathbf{\Gamma}:=(\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2},\gamma)
be a BD triple of type
A_{n-1}
and
G\in\{\SL_{n},\GL_{n}\}
. Then the following statements hold:

1)

The generalized cluster structure
\mathcal{GC}_{g}^{\dagger}(\mathbf{\Gamma},G)
is regular;

2)

All the variables in the initial extended cluster of
\mathcal{GC}_{g}^{\dagger}(\mathbf{\Gamma},G)
are irreducible (as elements of
\mathcal{O}(G)
) and (for cluster variables) coprime with their mutations;

3)

The generalized cluster structure
\mathcal{GC}_{g}^{\dagger}(\mathbf{\Gamma},G)
is complete.

Proof.

Let
\Psi_{0}
be the initial extended cluster of
\mathcal{GC}_{g}^{\dagger}(\mathbf{\Gamma},G)
. In the case of
\mathbf{\Gamma}=\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\text{std}}
, the proof can be executed in the same way as in
[
20
]
for
\mathcal{GC}_{h}^{\dagger}(\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\text{std}},G)
. There, the proof was based on various normal forms of a generic element
U\in\GL_{n}
. For instance, in the case of the initial extended cluster of
\mathcal{GC}_{h}^{\dagger}(\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\text{std}},G)
, one decomposes
U
as
U=U_{\oplus}U_{-}
and as
U=N_{-}B_{+}C\,\hskip-3.0pt\left.N_{-}\hskip-4.0pt\right.^{-1}
for some
N_{-},U_{-}\in\mathcal{N}_{-}
,
U_{\oplus},B_{+}\in\mathcal{B}_{+}
and
C:=e_{1n}+\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}e_{i+1,i}
. Then
\varphi(U)=\varphi(B_{+})
and
h(U)=h(U_{\oplus})
for
\varphi
- and
h
-variables. In the case of
\mathcal{GC}_{g}^{\dagger}(\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\text{std}},G)
, one decomposes
U
as
U=U_{+}U_{\ominus}
and
U=N_{+}B_{-}C^{T}\,\hskip-3.0pt\left.N_{+}\hskip-4.0pt\right.^{-1}
, where
N_{+},U_{+}\in\mathcal{N}_{+}
and
U_{\ominus},B_{-}\in\mathcal{B}_{-}
. Then, to express
u_{ij}
in terms of
\mathcal{L}_{\mathbb{C}}(\Psi_{0})
, it is enough to express the entries of
B_{-}
and
U_{\ominus}
in terms of
\mathcal{L}_{\mathbb{C}}(\Psi_{0})
. This process is described in
[
20
]
. For
|\Gamma_{1}|=1
, one checks the conditions of Proposition
2.28
for the marked variable, which in turn can be transferred from the case of
\mathcal{GC}_{h}^{\dagger}(\mathbf{\Gamma},G)
via the transposition map. For
|\Gamma_{1}|>1
, the proof proceeds along the same lines as in the case of
\mathcal{GC}_{h}^{\dagger}(\mathbf{\Gamma})
, and it is based on the inductive argument on the size of
|\Gamma_{1}|
and Proposition
2.30
, which in turn relies on the existence of birational quasi-isomorphisms
\mathcal{G}^{\mathrm{op}}
with distinct sets of marked variables.
∎

Let us recall that the number
k_{\mathbf{\Gamma}}
is given by
k_{\mathbf{\Gamma}}=|\Pi\setminus\Gamma_{1}|
.

Proposition 7.11
.

Let
\mathbf{\Gamma}
be a BD triple of type
A_{n-1}
. The following statements hold:

1)

The action of
\mathcal{H}_{\mathbf{\Gamma}}
upon
\SL_{n}
induces a global toric action upon
\mathcal{GC}_{g}^{\dagger}(\mathbf{\Gamma},\SL_{n})
of rank
k_{\mathbf{\Gamma}}
;

2)

The action of
\mathcal{H}_{\mathbf{\Gamma}}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}
upon
\GL_{n}
induces a global toric action upon
\mathcal{GC}_{g}^{\dagger}(\mathbf{\Gamma},\GL_{n})
of rank
k_{\mathbf{\Gamma}}+1
.

Proof.

The fact that the action by scalars
\mathbb{C}^{*}
induces a global toric action upon
\mathcal{GC}_{g}^{\dagger}(\mathbf{\Gamma},\GL_{n})
can be proved in the same way as in the case of
\mathcal{GC}_{h}^{\dagger}(\mathbf{\Gamma},\GL_{n})
(see Lemma
4.21
). Due to Proposition
4.16
and the commutative diagram (
3.55
),
\mathcal{Q}^{\mathrm{op}}:(G,\pi_{\text{std}}^{\dagger})\dashrightarrow(G,\pi_{\mathbf{\Gamma}}^{\dagger})
is equivariant with respect to the action of
\mathcal{H}_{\mathbf{\Gamma}}
; as a consequence, the
g
-functions are semi-invariant with respect to the action of
\mathcal{H}_{\mathbf{\Gamma}}
. Furthermore, due to the semi-invariance of the
\varphi
-functions and Remark
7.3
, the
\phi
-functions are also semi-invariant with respect to the action; hence,
\mathcal{H}_{\mathbf{\Gamma}}
induces a local toric action upon the initial extended cluster
\Psi_{0}
of
\mathcal{GC}_{g}^{\dagger}(\mathbf{\Gamma})
. Due to Proposition
7.5
, Remark
7.3
and Proposition
5.11
, all the
y
-variables in
\Psi_{0}
are invariant with respect to
\mathcal{H}_{\mathbf{\Gamma}}
except
y(\phi_{kl})
,
k+l=n
, which require a separate check. In turn, this reduces to showing that
\pi_{0}([\nabla_{U}\log y(\phi_{kl}),U])=0
for
\mathbf{\Gamma}=\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\text{std}}
. Set
\Delta(i,j):=\sum_{k=i}^{j}e_{kk}
. It is not difficult to see that

\displaystyle\pi_{0}([\nabla_{U}\log\phi_{kl},U])=\Delta(1,l)-\Delta(k+1,n)+(n-k-l)\Delta(1,1);

(7.28)

\displaystyle\pi_{0}([\nabla_{U}\log g_{ij},U])=\Delta(j,n-i+j)-\Delta(i,n).

(7.29)

Now, the neighborhoods of
\phi_{kl}
for
k+l=n
are given by Figure
15b
, Figure
16b
and Figure
17b
, and it is a matter of a direct computation that
\pi_{0}([\nabla_{U}\log y(\phi_{kl}),U])=0
,
k+l=n
. The Casimirs
\hat{p}_{ir}
from the statement of Proposition
2.19
are the same as in
\mathcal{GC}_{h}^{\dagger}(\mathbf{\Gamma})
, hence they are invariant with respect to
\mathcal{H}_{\mathbf{\Gamma}}
. It follows from Proposition
2.19
that the local toric action of
\mathcal{H}_{\mathbf{\Gamma}}
upon
\mathcal{GC}_{g}^{\dagger}(\mathbf{\Gamma})
is global. To verify that the action is of rank
k_{\mathbf{\Gamma}}
, one applies the same argument as in Proposition
5.11
based on Proposition
7.10
.
∎

Lemma 7.12
.

Let
g_{ij}
be a
g
-function and
\phi_{kl}
be a
\phi
-function from the initial extended cluster of
\mathcal{GC}_{g}^{\dagger}(\mathbf{\Gamma})
. Then

\{g_{ij},\phi_{kl}\}_{\mathbf{\Gamma}}^{\dagger}=\langle R_{0}\pi_{0}[\nabla_{U}g_{ij},U],[\nabla_{U}\phi_{kl},U]\rangle-\langle\pi_{0}(U\nabla_{U}g_{ij}),[\nabla_{U}\phi_{kl},U]\rangle.

(7.30)

Moreover, the
g
-functions are log-canonical with the
\phi
-functions.

Proof.

Set
\mathring{\nu}^{*}(g_{ij}):=U\nabla_{U}g_{ij}-\mathring{\gamma}^{*}(\nabla_{U}g_{ij}\cdot U)
. Due to the invariance property (
7.7
),
\mathring{\nu}^{*}(g_{ij})\in\mathfrak{b}_{+}
; therefore,
